## Part B — the round

### R7-01 The family round: scope, supersession by name, both ends, the blind spots, grading

**Governs:** every issue of round 7.

**Decision.** (1) **Scope** (J144). This round rules the 53 round-6 findings of the raised-way × wall family
(`work/round7-work/`, 28 major, 25 minor) and nothing else; the other 28 are held for round 8 (listed at the end, no
ruling). The loop is: this adjudication, a probe until dry (up to three passes, each decided into a Part P of this file),
the family's repair, then family-focused verify — repeated until a focused verify finds no major in the family.
(2) **Both ends and the five blind spots.** Each seat applies every FIX and END line under its heading in the same round
as the owning seat's share, and greps its own unit for every term, field, key, string key and section the rulings change,
in prose, tables, edge rows, tests, fixture lists, milestone cells and index rows alike, conforming every restatement it
finds in the same block and naming each in its report; one it missed goes to its HAND-OFFS. The sweep covers the round's
five blind spots: (a) V07's village restatements; (b) V09's panels and V10's clerk; (c) the tests, fixture lists and
milestone cells; (d) the base's summaries and index rows (§4.1's stage rows, §4.13, §5.3, §5.4, §7.4, §7.7, §13.8, §14);
(e) V11's registry readers and V12's statistics. This adjudicator's sweep of all five is the END lines of Part H.
(3) **Cite, never restate**: a site that restated a rule R7-02 homes is reduced to a citation of the home, with at most
the outcome's or sentence's name. (4) A unit whose heading holds END lines but owns no issue runs: **scope, model,
pipeline, determinism, rendering, V04, V05, V06, V07, V09, V10, V11** each own issues; **V01** owns one; (J146) **V12** owns none but holds E-V12-1 and runs; **V02, V03, V08** and **kernel** neither own an issue nor hold an END line this round and do not run. (5) **Supersession by
name.** This round supersedes, by J144 and R7-02: **R6-02**'s (D5) lock-row parenthesis, its C8 column description and
cells, its bent-group list in *beneath* ("A stretch laid by neither … is bent locally …"), its *No gate and no tower*
clause as it stood, W2's and W13's event cells, W8's C2 cell ("once the record is dropped"), the way-and-channel table's
row (c) outcome and row (e)'s report clause, and (7)'s extension sentence where R7-04 adds to it; **V04 §3.11's UC18**
for a wall coming to stand over an arch vault's span (UC18 stands for every generator and every other op); **V06 §3.19
(5)**'s impassable list as it applies to a locked, pinned or declared wall or ground way; and **every clause of the set
that makes a lock or pin refuse, skip or alter a meeting between a wall and a raised way** (the "locked, pinned or
neither" clauses keep their words where they say the outcome is the same; R7-02's lock sentence is their home). Every
other text of R6-02 and its Part P stands as amended below. (6) No seat re-raises an issue ruled INVALID or MERGED in
adjudication-r2 – r6 or here.

**Grading (J141).** As R6-01 grades: an untested edge whose outcome the text defines but no bar runs, so that a wrong build
passes every bar, is MAJOR; a test, fixture list or cell that omits or misnames a case whose outcome is the same by
construction is MINOR; two units stating two outcomes for one case is MAJOR however rare. Every reported grade of the 53
stands under this standard; no regrade (the reasons are in Part H's notes where a grade could be doubted).

## Part L — the family's laws (J146; normative)

> (J146) The owner chose laws plus examples (PROGRESS J146; the owner's words: "Laws + examples (Recommended)"). This
> Part is the raised-way × wall family's **normative** text; its words are the program's (J122, vetoable — the restated
> veto list at its end names each behaviour a builder would see). R7-02 (its lock, bent-group and fixed-place sentences,
> the crossing table and the way-and-channel table), R7-03 – R7-07 and Part P's amendments are **worked examples** of
> these laws: where an example and the laws differ, the laws govern; a case no example lists is decided by the laws.
> **Grading in this family** (J146, J141's standard as the owner applies it here): a finding is MAJOR only where the
> laws give two outcomes or none for a case, contradict an owner rule, or no bar checks an invariant of law 1; an
> example that differs from the laws, or a case missing from the examples, is never a major — at most a minor, the
> example corrected to the laws. **Home:** §2.7's support step (6), stated there once, from **Terms** through **The
> check** below, as **§2.7's laws** (the model's END E-M1); every other text cites "§2.7's laws" with the law's number
> and never restates them. Nothing below changes an owner rule: J144's rule (a lock holds placement, not shape) is law
> 2's *Entry* and *The soft readings* (J148); item 110 (b) and (f) are law 1 (1)–(3); item 114 is kept (a run's
> condition is its own, law 1 (2), (7)–(8) reading it); item 101 R2's nearest lawful alternative is law 2 (c). (J147)
> The owner's J147 (drawing or dragging holds nothing; locks have two axes, placement and shape; a shape lock is
> honoured where a raised way meets a wall) is *held* — locked, on the axis named, and nothing else, J144's rule the
> placement lock's — and law 1 (10). (J148) The owner's J148 (a gate does not move; three tiers, the owner's choice) is
> *the tiers* and law 2's order: the newer planned round the hard ones, then the soft ones moved, then the op giving
> way. (J149) The owner's J149 (an aqueduct crosses a wall at its own level) is law 1 (2), (5) and (6), **over** and
> **merged** written and **beneath** left out (*J149 applied*). (J150) The owner's J150 keeps one plain shape-lock rule
> (law 1 (10)), hands a lock's behaviour under an undo, a redo, a re-grade or value edit within a standing meeting, and
> a repair to round 8 (Part A), and stops Part L growing. The texts of J147 – J150's readings, tiers and forms are the
> program's (J122). (J146) Refined by class, never by case, against probe passes 4 – 7 (Part P), law 1 (2)'s forms
> restored to J149's level (PRB7F-GEN-03) and a drawn carried way to J147 (1) (PRB7G-GEN-01), each flagged there.

**Terms** — each defined once, as a function of what stands when it is read; where several raised ways stand, law 1
reads each one's own strip, ends, zone, deck line and spans, a thing lawful only where it is so against each, and a move
or search keeping a thing clear of them reads their union (J146, PRB7D-TIM-03; PRB7G-LCK-04, PRB7G-GEN-05).
* A **meeting** is any contact of a **wall-like structure** — an enceinte of any kind (a town or stage ring, a castle's
  curtain and inner ward, a citadel, a village's palisade, earthwork or inner circuit), a quay wall or a retaining wall,
  with its runs, gates (posterns, water gates and a harbour-mouth chain among them), towers, moat and the ways its own
    law lays (an intervallum, a quay, a castle's paths) — with a **raised way** — a viaduct or an elevated road (each a
  **road deck**) or an aqueduct, with its supports, ramps, stairs, feet, spans, arch vaults and the way it carries (by
  hand, the declared way to its stroke's end; a generated road deck's, the route it was priced on, past each foot —
  J146, PRB7E-GEN-05). An **enclosing wall** is every wall-like structure but a retaining wall.
* **What stands of a wall** is read run by run (J146, PRB7D-TIM-02; PRB7E-TIM-01). Each run of a wall record stands,
  whatever its condition, with its line, its band, its wall strips and the gates, towers and fixed places on it — save a
  run its record no longer has (a hand `delete` of a run, or a burial of the runs it lists, V06 §3.20.5 (4), cause
  `rule:burial`) and every run of a record tombstoned, `converted` by `wallToWay` (its line the ring road's spine, a
  way) or cleared by any cause but `absorbWall`, which stand nowhere; and a run of a record `absorbWall` cleared (cause
  `convert:absorbWall`, V06 §2.8), which stands while its condition is not `dismantled`, with its band as it stood and
  its wall strips merged into the blocks. A gate `gateToJunction` converted is a junction, no gate. Every wall, line,
  band, wall strip, run, gate, tower and fixed place these laws read is of what stands.
* **The band** of a wall at a point is its section there by the rows of the construction that laid it or last
  re-sectioned it (its derivation record, §2.8) — V07 §3.4's for an enceinte laid by VS3's construction (VS3 at a
  village derivation, and every op that runs VS3's form at a village rung: a village kind's `drawWall`, by hand or for a
  dossier wall record, and `repairWall`'s new stretch, V07's edit table) or rebuilt by V07 §3.11's REPLACEMENT (the
  palisade's stakes, the earthwork's bank with its ditch, the inner circuit's wall), and §4.8's along its stretch
  profile for every other (a town's `inner` ring, and a village kind drawn at a TOWN rung or above, among them), at
  whatever rung each stands, so no change of product or rung alters a band or wall strips (J146, PRB7D-TIM-04;
  PRB7E-TIM-03) —; a retaining wall's band is its line. **The wall's top** at a point is its base there raised by its
  **height** there, as its section's rows give it, whatever its runs' condition, law 1 (7)'s cap a reading beside it
  (J146, PRB7G-GEN-03, PRB7G-TIM-01). **The wall strips** are the band with its inner setback inside and, outside, its
  outer setback with its glacis — on a moated stretch its berm up to the moat (§4.8); **their width** at a place is the
  band's half there plus the wider of the inner setback and the outer setback with its glacis, read so whether or not a
  moat runs or will run there (a berm never narrowing it), and for a band V07 §3.4 gives `palisade.bandM` from its line;
  where two walls' lines meet or run together (a castle's welded stretch and weld vertices, a stretch anchored on an
  earlier enceinte) the band and the width there are the wider of the two walls' (J146, PRB7D-GEN-04, PRB7D-TIM-04).
* **The strip** of a raised way is its under-deck strip — the deck's plan between its feet widened each side by
  `raisedWays.structure.stripMarginM` (§4.6) — read from its record whatever its spans' and supports' `state`; **a
  span's strip** is the span rule's, the deck's plan between its two piers' inner faces without that margin (§2.7's span
  rule (1)), law 1 (8) and (9) reading each span's own (J146, PRB7D-LCK-14); **the ends** are its ramps, stairs and feet,
  each widened by the same margin. A **standing span** is one whose `state` is absent, a **standing support** one whose
  `state` is absent or `ruined` (J146, PRB7D-TIM-06); a `cleared` one's ring is open ground. A point of a road deck's
  carried way past its foot is **beyond the foot** where a wall's line, band and wall strips and a gate's footprint there
  are all clear of the ends and the strip; the nearest such point is **the first point beyond the foot**, which every
  rule that places or moves a crossing or a gate there takes (S5a's pin, the bent group's (i)) (J146, PRB7E-GEN-03).
* A **footprint** is a tower's (`Tower.footprint`, an end post's, a retaining wall's decorative tower's), a gate's (its
  gatehouse with its passage, else its passage across the band; a harbour-mouth chain's water gate across the entrance
  with the tower at each head) and a fixed place's (its own points — `Gate.banks`, an anchor, a weld vertex — with the
  footprint standing there).
* A **fixed place** is a place a wall's own law ties to something other than the spacing along its line, so that it is
  laid only along that tie: a river's water gate (tied to the river), an anchor with its tower (to the barrier's edge),
  a harbour-mouth chain (to the heads it spans), and a castle's weld vertices, town-side gate and vertex towers (to its
  claim, which moves only whole, by its siting). Every other gate, postern and tower is laid along its wall's line by
  the law that lays it; none, once standing, is moved by a meeting (J148). **The zone** of a road deck, for a fixed
  place of a wall, is its strip widened each side by that wall's strips' width at that place — at a place off the
  wall's line (a harbour-mouth chain across an entrance the trace does not reach), the width at the point of the line
  nearest it (J146, PRB7E-GEN-10); an aqueduct has none (law 1 (6); J149).
* **The tiers** (J148: the owner's rule and choice; the tier texts the program's). **Tier 1** — gates (posterns, water
  gates and a harbour-mouth chain among them), towers and the fixed places — is never moved by a meeting (law 1 (11)). **Tier 2** —
  wall runs (their lines, bands and strips) and raised ways (their courses, supports, spans and ends) — is hard: where
  two meet, the newer bends itself locally. **Tier 3** — ground ways (a wall's own ways and a road deck's carried way
  among them), plots and unlocked buildings — is soft, moved round the hard ones. A held building and anything
  shape-locked is moved by no meeting (law 1 (9), (10)); every new thing first plans round the hard ones (law 2 (a)).
* A **held building's strip** is the strip of a `vault` span whose chamber seats a held building (one seated there, V04
  §3.5.4 (d), and locked on either axis — J147), widened all round by the meeting wall's strips' width.
* **Anything across a strip** is whatever is laid on the ground within it, and wet ground: a wall's band or wall strips,
  a run that stands, a retaining wall's line, a way's corridor, water, a flood cell or marsh, a square or plaza, a
  reservation other than a raised way's, a plot or building, a grove; and, of another raised way, its supports, ramps,
  stairs and feet and, across a span whose deck was fixed after that raised way's there (*Order*), its whole under-deck
  strip, whatever its spans' reading. A raised way's own reservation (its supports, ends and `vault` spans' under-deck
  strips, §2.7's support step (6)) and its own arch vault with what the chamber seats (a declared placement's plot and
  building, V04 §3.5.4 (d)), its door, links and accesses are never across its own strips, so no span's reading turns
  on its own chamber or on a raised way fixed after it (J146, PRB7E-GEN-01, PRB7E-LCK-06, PRB7E-TIM-02). **Open ground**
  — ground holding only its terrain and a dry land use, a `cleared` support's ring among it — is nothing across (J146,
  PRB7D-TIM-03).
* **Held** is locked, on the axis named, and nothing else (J147): **held in placement** is placement-locked — the lock
  the user makes (T10 `hold`, UC18), a pin among them — and holds where a thing is, its shape still changing at a
  meeting (J144); **held in shape** is shape-locked and holds its outline and size (*shape*, below), its cosmetics still
  following the neighbourhood (law 1 (10); J150). A lock on a wall-like structure or a raised way holds it, on its axis,
  with its parts (its runs, gates, posterns and towers; its supports, spans and arch vaults), so at a regeneration they
  stand with it before every stage. A thing drawn, dragged, declared or placed by hand holds nothing unless locked: it
  keeps its existence and meets as an unlocked thing of its kind. The support step's keeping of every raised way's
  stations, keys and spans from its creation (§2.7's support step (6)) is no hold (J146, PRB7D-GEN-05; J147).
* **Placement** is where a structure is, as its record's fields hold it — a wall-like structure's trace (its line as a
  curve, a vertex inserted on it without moving it changing none) with the place of each gate, postern and tower
  standing on it (a gate newly laid moving none; J146, PRB7F-LCK-02, PRB7G-LCK-03), a raised way's course, deck line,
  stations, keys and feet, an arch vault's span and key, a ground way's two ends; **shape** is what a meeting may change
  of a standing thing — the soft readings (law 2), a gate *Carried way* makes in it, an arch vault's chamber and a
  Tier-3 thing's local course (law 2 (b)) — and, for a shape lock, its outline and size besides: a wall-like structure's
  section, band and width, a raised way's deck width and its spans' `use`, a building's footprint and plot (J146,
  PRB7D-LCK-08; J147; J148; PRB7E-GEN-09). **The reach** of a meeting is the strips, zones, ends and Tier-1 footprints
  it meets and, along each line through them, the nearest points clear of them.
* **Order** (J146, PRB7D-GEN-06, PRB7D-LCK-09; PRB7E-GEN-02). Lines are fixed in one order: a creation prefix's
  declarations in log order; then a generation run's, a regeneration's or a village derivation's steps in the order it
  runs them — S3's numbered steps, S4, then for each stage k in turn its S5a boundary, S5b, S5a trace, S5c and S5d, then
  S6 onward; VS3 and the village steps in theirs —;
  then each later event in log order, an epoch's or a replay's ops in its op order (an epoch's new walled stage running
  its S5 steps in that order inside its op); two lines one step fixes are ordered by address, the lower first. Each run
  of an enclosing wall records the order of the step that fixed its line (`Enceinte.laidAt`), and each stretch of a
  raised way's deck line that of the step that fixed its course (`RaisedWayRecord.courseAt`) — stretch-state profiles
  of `{ seq, step }`, `seq` the event's place in that order and `step` its step's place in the order the event runs its
  steps (0 by hand), a knot reading its earlier side. A line is dated by the step that fixes it, whichever step commits
  it: a stage's trace by the step that picked its boundary (S3 (6) for stage 1, whose trace S5a commits for k = 1; stage
  k's S5a boundary, before its own S5b), a castle's curtain, inner ward and arcs by its claim (S3 (8), or a stage
  citadel's trace batch), a raised stretch by the step that fixed its course (S3 (5)'s gate routes' by S3 (7)). The op
  that lays or moves a line writes its order — a re-shape, a warp, a `revert`, a re-trace and an extension bent past a
  bound date what they move —; every other op keeps it — a re-section on its kept trace, a Deck height or Arch spacing
  edit, an extension that keeps its course and a repair (the line they continue), a `split` or `merge` (each piece its
  own), an undo or a redo (each restoring or re-applying its target's record; J146, PRB7F-LCK-01), and at a
  regeneration a structure it keeps where it stands. A stretch a commit moves keeps its former order at each crossing
  it had as the commit began and still has (law 1 (3); J146, PRB7F-LCK-04).
* A **commit** (J146, PRB7D-TIM-01, PRB7D-GEN-01) is one event with every batch it chains (§5.3), the unit at whose end
  law 1 holds: by hand an edit, V04's arch-vault reconcile in it; an epoch — a replay's and a village's among them —
  from its first batch through `afterGrowth`'s `under.extend`, whose reconcile lays and removes chambers (V06 §3.2); at
  generation, a regeneration and a village derivation each stage (§3.3's `StageId`), law 1 holding at its stage end, the
  arch vaults laid by the underground tail's vaults step (V04's T2) after the first map. Every op resolves the meetings
  it makes by laws 2–3 as it acts, inside its commit. What stood **as a commit began** is read by hand and at an epoch
  from its undo record (§7.4 *Application*), at a stage from what the stages before left (the first's, the records the
  event keeps); a meeting standing at its end whose two things did not meet so then — a line, band or strip in a strip
  it did not lie in, a deck line across a line it did not cross, whichever side moved or grew — it makes **newly**, and
  one whose reading is deeper than then (a span read `open`; a merge, a cap or a lower soffit over a point) it
  **deepens**, no other being either, whatever order its fields hold (J146, PRB7F-LCK-01, PRB7F-GEN-01; PRB7G-LCK-02).

**LAW 1 — what a valid meeting is.** At the end of every commit, on every path, at every rung (J146, PRB7D-TIM-01):
1. no raised way has a junction with ground part 0: its deck shares no vertex or edge with a ground way but at its two
   ends, no gate stands on it, and a deck passing over a wall is no crossing of the gate law (item 110 (b), (f));
2. an enclosing wall's line crosses an aqueduct's deck line (law 1 (4)) at the aqueduct's level, in the form that level
   there gives, nothing of either record written (J149) — its levels read from the records, each span's soffit standing
   or fallen, whatever either side's condition (item 114; J146, PRB7G-TIM-01): **over**, where its channel there lies
   above the wall's top — no standing support in the band there and every span above the band clearing the wall's top,
   the band passing beneath the arch; **merged**, where its channel lies within the wall's height — the band built up to
   and around what stands of the aqueduct's arch or pier there (none standing, the band whole) at the wall's own top,
   the water passing through it in the channel, the arch no gate (no way passes it) and no gate, postern or tower in it
   (law 1 (5)); each keeping its own condition (item 114); a crossing that fits neither form is unlawful (J149; J146,
   PRB7F-GEN-03, PRB7F-TIM-02); so every aqueduct crosses every wall through an arch, over it or within it (item 110
   (b): "crossing the wall through an arch", as the program reads it); a retaining wall's line meets any deck line
   anywhere, the deck passing over it and a support on its line built against it;
3. a road deck's deck line crosses an enclosing wall's line only where that run's line was fixed after the deck line
   there (*Order*: its `laidAt` later than that stretch's `courseAt` — a stage's trace dated by its boundary's pick, so
   never at a boundary its stage picked before solving the deck's route), the band then built through each of the
   deck's supports it meets; across a run fixed before the deck line there, the deck comes down before it and its
   carried way crosses on ground part 0 through a gate beyond the foot (J146, PRB7D-GEN-06, PRB7D-LCK-09; PRB7E-GEN-02);
4. no enclosing wall's line, band or wall strips lie over a raised way's ends, and its band lies in a raised way's strip
   only to cross its deck line — entering on one long side, leaving by the other, meeting the deck line once there
   (J146, PRB7F-LCK-05);
5. no gate's, postern's or tower's footprint lies in a strip, under a standing span or a fallen one, but in an
   aqueduct's crossing law 1 (2) reads over whose every span over it clears its top, never on a support's ring (J149;
   J146, PRB7F-TIM-02);
6. no fixed place, with its footprint, lies in a road deck's zone, nor in an aqueduct's strip but where law 1 (5)
   admits a gate or tower there (J149);
7. wherever any part of a wall's band lies beneath a standing span of a road deck (or a standing support bearing one),
   its top and walk are no higher than the lowest road deck's soffit there, an aqueduct capping none (law 1 (2); J149;
   J146, PRB7G-GEN-05);
8. a `vault` span is read `open` while anything lies across its strip and `vault` while nothing does, its record
   unchanged either way; from the commit that lays its raised way's arch vaults on that path — by hand the edit's own,
   at an epoch its `under.extend`, at generation and a regeneration the underground tail's vaults step; never before the
   underground layer (M6-U), where the reading alone holds — a span read `vault` has its chamber (`collapsed` where V04
   §3.5.4 (d) (2) says) and a span read `open` none (J146, PRB7D-TIM-01, PRB7D-GEN-01);
9. a held building's chamber, footprint and door are unchanged by any meeting: nothing lies across the strip of the span
   whose chamber seats it, and no wall's line lies newly (*commit*) in the held building's strip of a building held at a
   commit's end (J146, PRB7D-LCK-15; J147; PRB7F-GEN-01; J150);
10. a thing held in placement keeps its placement and a thing held in shape its shape, changed by no op but one that
    names it — a meeting changing a placement-held thing's shape alone (J144) —, and no op makes or deepens (*commit*) a
    meeting with a shape-held thing: no wall-like line, band or strip newly across the strip of a `vault`-use span of
    it, no aqueduct merged into it, no soffit lowered over a run of it, no gate made in it (J147; J150, its behaviour
    under an undo, a redo, a re-grade or value edit within a standing meeting, or a repair round 8's to design, this
    rule reading each meanwhile, Part A; J146, PRB7E-LCK-04; PRB7F-LCK-01, PRB7F-GEN-01);
11. no gate, postern, tower or fixed place standing as a commit begins, laid by an earlier batch of it or fixed by an
    earlier step of its event (*Not yet built*) is moved or removed by a meeting: at its end each stands where it stood,
    was laid or was fixed, or where the op's own edit of it or of what carries it puts it (a gate the user places or
    slides, a warp, a re-section's re-derived footprint), save one the op removes, or lays again on a line its own law
    re-traces, by that law (a run deleted, buried, converted or dismantled; the run a moved vertex or a water op
    re-traces between the towers its own law leaves unaffected) (J148; J146, PRB7F-TIM-03, PRB7F-LCK-03; PRB7G-TIM-03,
    PRB7G-GEN-02).

**LAW 2 — one way to resolve any meeting**, whatever op makes it: (a), then (b), then (c), each tried only where the one
before leaves no lawful shape, for every op on every path (J146, PRB7D-LCK-01, PRB7D-GEN-02) — the newer bending itself
round the hard ones, then the soft ones moved, then the op giving way (J148).
* *Entry.* Whether an op may act on its own target at all is settled before any meeting: by hand by §7.1's lock row (a
  target the user locked refused, `refuse.locked`, as that row refuses one — its two axes round 8's (Part A); a target
  drawn, dragged, declared or placed by hand and not locked changed as an unlocked one is, J147), by the engine or a
  hazard by §7.2 *Protection*, V06 §3.1 and V05 §3.16 (2) (a part they protect never struck, decayed or covered). No
  placement lock or pin on a wall-like structure, a raised way or an arch vault refuses, skips or alters a meeting
  (J144: it holds placement, not shape); a shape lock is honoured (law 1 (10); J147; J150); a held building keeps its
  chamber (law 1 (9)).
* *Newer.* Of a meeting's two sides the newer is the op's own structure — the one it names as its target, creating or
  changing it; a structure it changes only at the meeting (a gate *Carried way* makes, a Tier-3 thing law 2 (b) moves, a
  soft reading) is the standing side; where the op names both or neither (a warp), the raised way is the older (its
  stations kept from its creation, §2.7's support step (6)) (J146, PRB7D-LCK-10, PRB7D-GEN-05; J148; PRB7G-LCK-01).
  Between structures standing before the op the newer is the later by *Order* where they meet — a creation prefix's
  declarations before every stage, and at a regeneration every structure it keeps where it stands (each locked one, on
  either axis, and each one §7.2 keeps so until round 8 rules the two-axis lock there, Part A) older than every stage,
  keeping its date, one it lays again taking the stage's (J147; J146, PRB7E-GEN-04). A meeting of two held structures
  stays as their last commit left it.
* *Not yet built.* A part not yet built when a step, law 1 or the check reads it, whose structure's course, place or
  section is already fixed — a gate's or fixed place's footprint, a band's width, a wall's top and its gates' and
  towers' heights before S7 writes them, the stations and spans of a raised stretch whose course S3 (7) fixed, a
  castle's curtain from its claim's commit — is read as the rows that build it will build it from what stands then, and
  the step that builds it builds exactly that; a part whose place the step that lays it chooses by its own law — every
  tower and postern S7 lays, at a turn vertex or by spacing, but a fixed place's — is that step's own, the newer when it
  is laid (J146, PRB7F-GEN-04; PRB7G-GEN-02, PRB7G-GEN-03).
* *The soft readings* (J148). A `vault` span read `open` with no chamber (law 1 (8)), a wall merged round an aqueduct's
  arch or pier (law 1 (2)) and a run's top capped beneath a road deck's span (law 1 (7)) are no move: each follows of
  itself from what stands, at every step — whichever side's op puts something across a strip or takes the last thing off
  it, a chamber following at the commit's end (J146, PRB7D-TIM-01) —, so law 3 never ranks them.
* *Carried way.* A road deck's carried way meets every enclosing wall on its ground course by the gate law (§5.3's gate
  row), its gate at the first point beyond the foot: by hand §7.3's S7 operator makes it in every wall the declared way
  crosses, a placement-locked one too, inserted on the wall's standing line, which it neither re-traces nor moves (a
  gate the op lays, standing from its commit as every gate, kept when its carried way goes; the wall's shape change
  shown by law 4's (G2); J146, PRB7F-LCK-02, PRB7G-LCK-03); off the hand path only the gate law of a stage that lays its
  trace after the route is solved makes one (S3 (7)'s, S5b's), never in a trace laid before, whose standing gates the
  route takes — a carried way standing before the stage (a creation prefix's, or one a regeneration keeps where it
  stands, *Newer*) taking its gate where that stage's boundary crosses its ground course or ends (at stage 1 S3 (7)'s
  once S3 (6) has fixed the boundary, a gate beside `N₁` as the harbour gate is; at a stage k ≥ 2 a pin of S5a, one of
  S5b's `c`), none where the boundary crosses only its deck (law 1 (3); J146, PRB7F-GEN-05, PRB7G-GEN-01). Where the
  wall is shape-locked, or its gate law gives the way no lawful gate there, the carried way — a Tier-3 way, unless
  itself shape-locked — is re-routed by law 2 (b) from the first point beyond the foot through the nearest gate of that
  wall that a way of its class may pass (§4.8 *Gates*: no postern, water gate or chain), never past its ends, else the
  op takes law 2 (c) (J147; J148, PRB7E-LCK-03, PRB7F-LCK-07; J144, J147 (1): PRB7G-GEN-01). A generated deck's route is
  solved again by its stage and meets the deck by the way-and-channel table's row (a) and law 1 (3), a lock on the deck
  holding its parts, never its route (J146, PRB7D-GEN-07; PRB7E-GEN-05).
* *A warp* is one op: each step is applied by §3.6, whose halving answers the kernel's own checks and never these laws,
  and its result is a given shape law 2 resolves before law 1 is checked at the commit's end (J146, PRB7D-LCK-18).

(a) The newer structure is laid lawfully first — law 1 holding and every rule of its own derivation, §4.3's crossing
    rule among them for a way, a gate's too, laid beneath a standing raised way, never for a raised way laid over a
    standing way (J146, PRB7F-LCK-06; PRB7G-GEN-04) —, within its own reach, planned round every Tier-1 thing law 1 (11)
    holds that it does not lay again, one its own edit carries among them (PRB7G-TIM-03, PRB7G-GEN-02), and the other
    side's Tier-2 structure (J148): each thing it lays — newly, or again on a run its own law re-traces (law 1 (11);
    J146, PRB7F-LCK-03) — or places — a gate, postern or tower of its own along its own wall's line, a fixed place along
    its tie, a crossing along the line it crosses, a station along its deck, an end it lays back along its own line
    (J146, PRB7F-LCK-04) — moved along that line or tie, and each stretch of its own line, course or way it changes
    re-laid only locally, between the nearest points clear of what it meets on either side (a bent-group move, a
    re-section's local re-trace, a course bent clear of a gate or tower) (J146, PRB7E-LCK-01); every change no further
    than the meeting's nearest clear points (*the reach*), within the bound its own law gives it (the pull's search
    band, the support step's slide bound, `wall.barrierReachM` for an anchor, a siting's candidates, an edited value's
    declared range) and never past its line's ends (J146, PRB7D-LCK-02): an op whose event sets the line, point or value
    it changes (a stroke, a drag of a vertex, gate or grip — a castle's among them —, an edit's value, a warp's result)
    takes the lawful shape law 3 picks nearest it, and every other (a pull or cost field, a route solve, a siting, a
    points-form `drawWall`'s trace) searches only shapes law 1 admits, its own cost choosing among them (J146,
    PRB7G-LCK-06); (a) fails where none lies within that reach.
(b) Failing that, the soft give way to the hard (J148): the op's shape is taken as (a) would take it with those Tier-3
    things passable that (b) may move — a ground way not shape-locked (a wall's own way, a road deck's carried way), a
    plot, an unlocked building —, every other thing as impassable to it as to (a) (J146, PRB7E-LCK-02, PRB7E-GEN-06),
    a searched shape by its own cost, a given one by law 3; and each such thing that shape meets is moved by its own law
    within its own reach (G1; J146, PRB7E-LCK-03): a ground way re-routed by the router that laid it (§4.3; by hand §7.3's S4
    operator) between its nearest unmoved nodes either side (a carried way as *Carried way* says) — a placement-locked one's local course a shape change
    shown by law 4's (G2) —, a plot re-cut (S10 on the touched blocks), an unlocked building moved as its kind's are.
    Never a Tier-1 thing, a Tier-2 structure's line, band, supports, spans or ends, a held building or anything
    shape-locked (law 1 (9), (10)); the soft readings follow as at (a).
(c) Failing that, the op gives way (G3), neither side moved or broken. **By hand:** refused, `refuse.raised.meeting`,
    offering the nearest lawful declarations (item 101 R2; `PreviewResult.alternatives`) and **Show me why** — never an
    Unlock (no placement lock decides a meeting). **At generation, a regeneration and a village derivation:** the newer
    structure is not built — a raised way dropped, reported; a castle's claim passed for the siting's next candidate,
    then F-CASTLE's ladder — or, where its stage must lay it, that stage's raised-way rung runs alone (F-WALL's;
    F-V-RAISED at a village; J146, PRB7F-TIM-01): re-laid once with law 1's exclusions impassable and its search band
    widened to `wall.barrierReachM` beyond the stage's boundary (§4.4 S5a (1)'s reach to a barrier; at a village VS3's),
    so that it may pass round the raised way (J146, PRB7E-GEN-08), else the stage left unwalled, which then reads as
    §4.2 S3 (9) (a)'s unwalled stage — no `Enceinte` or `Gate` recorded for it, its gate routes and spawned primaries
    ways crossing its boundary with no gate, no harbour gate, moat or chain along it, the harbour route an unwalled
    town's, a castle welded to it re-sited by F-CASTLE else kept `inside`, the wall listed in `requestedVsBuilt` and
    `W_RAISED_MEETING` reported. **At an epoch and in a replay:** the op waits that epoch (`walls.waited`,
    `raised.waited`), a water op (`shiftCourse`, an avulsion, `digCanal`) being checked on its new water before the
    water is inserted and waiting likewise where the run its own law re-traces has no lawful shape, never widened past
    it (F-REDERIVE's rung; law 1 (11); J146, PRB7F-TIM-01); the one op that cannot wait is a frame growth, whose
    extension keeps the deck's course and, wherever a wall-like structure crosses the old end span, that span as it
    stood (the old foot's point its first new station), and past a bound no fitting course meets places each station off
    every Tier-1 footprint and every other obstacle but the one it fails, reported (`raised.extendedPastBound`; J146,
    PRB7G-TIM-02).

**LAW 3 — one total order** (J146, PRB7D-LCK-11, PRB7D-LCK-12, PRB7D-LCK-13, PRB7D-GEN-08). Among the lawful shapes law
2 (a) or (b) leaves open, the first by these keys in turn. (1) **What changes:** a shape that keeps the value the op set
comes before one that bends it (no lawful shape re-lays its course or trace past law 2 (a)'s local reach; J146,
PRB7E-LCK-01; PRB7F-LCK-04). (2) **Least displacement:** the value's difference; then the largest distance any changed
thing moves; then the sum of those distances; then the area between each re-laid line and its old — a thing moved
along a line (a gate, postern or tower the op lays or places, along its own wall's line; a gate the op's own wall lays
on a standing way, along that way; a fixed place the op lays, along its tie; a crossing along the deck line it crosses;
a station along its deck) measured along that line (J148), a thing the op itself drags measured back along its drag,
so that it stops on the side it came from, and a re-laid or re-routed line, course or way by the largest distance of
the new from the old. (3) **A canonical key** no two distinct shapes share: their changes, listed in the address order
of what each changes, compared item by item — the lower address, then the smaller value, then the point (a moved
thing's new place; a re-laid line's vertices in order, a prefix first) first by x, then by y, in the arena's frame. A
line's part inside a strip that crosses no deck line there goes to the strip's edge on the side it entered from. A
bend that would leave the op with no effect is no lawful shape of (a): law 2 (b) is tried, then (c) (J146,
PRB7D-LCK-03).

**LAW 4 — every op re-checks.** Every op on every path that changes the geometry, kind, section, width, class,
condition, existence or held state of a wall-like structure or a raised way, or anything law 1 (8) reads across a strip
— each stage, epoch, replay or village op (a burial and a silting among them), each hand tool (a castle's grip, a
terrain stroke, a retaining wall's chain grip, a terrace level), a warp, a revert, a split or merge, a width, class or
table edit (PRB7G-TIM-05), a skeleton's import and an undo — re-checks law 1 for the meetings it touches and resolves
them by laws 2–3 as it acts, law 1 holding at its commit's end (J146, PRB7D-TIM-01). An undo or a redo is its event's
exact inverse or re-application, never bent: where law 1 would not then hold it is refused (§7.4 *Independence*:
`refuse.undo.dependent`), naming each later live event whose structure or lock it would meet to undo first, else what it
would meet (J146, PRB7D-LCK-17; PRB7F-LCK-01; a lock's part J150's, law 1 (10)). **(G2)** Every change a meeting makes
to the shape of a thing held in placement — a placement-locked structure, part or way other than the op's own target, or
a placement-locked part of the op's own (an arch vault; J148: no meeting moves a gate or tower) — is shown, by hand as
the fate **reshaped** (`EditFate.fate` `'reshaped'`, with `met`, the structure it met, and `change`, as §5.4's
`reshaped` entry's), off the hand path as a `reshaped` entry in the report's changes (§5.4), and the event's undo
restores it. Every other change of the op's own structure is its bend line, never a fate; every change of an unheld
structure at a meeting (an unheld chamber removed or laid again among them) is one of the op's consequences (§7.3 item
4), never a fate (J146, PRB7D-LCK-06, PRB7D-LCK-07, PRB7D-LCK-08).

**The check.** Law 1 is checked at runtime by one §5.3 row at the end of every commit, each clause from what the commit
leaves, its event and what stood as it began (*commit*) — its spatial clauses by query between every part the commit
touched (a wall-like structure, gate, tower, fixed place, raised way, arch vault or held building; for (8) and (9),
every span whose own strip meets anything the commit laid, moved, re-classed or removed) and every part of the other
side it meets, touched or not: a raised way against every wall-like structure, gate, tower, fixed place and held
building, and these against the union of every standing raised way's strips, zones, ends and deck lines and each span's
own strip, whatever the op (J146, PRB7E-TIM-05; PRB7F-GEN-02); (3) by the stored *Order* of the lines that cross; (9)
and (10) by the meetings the commit made newly or deepened, (11) by what it moved or removed, against what stood as it
began or, for a thing laid or fixed earlier in its event, the place its laying batch or fixing step recorded (its
`Gate`, `Tower` or `Castle` record, or the stage plan S3 (6)–(9) fixed); (10)'s placement by each held structure the
commit touched — its placement as the event began, and a shape-held one's outline and size, unchanged unless the event
names it as its own target (a generation, a regeneration and a village derivation naming none) (J146, PRB7D-LCK-16,
PRB7D-GEN-06; J147; PRB7E-GEN-09; PRB7G-LCK-02, PRB7G-LCK-03, PRB7G-TIM-03); (11)'s exceptions read from the event (its
target, its edit, the run its own law re-traces) — and by one property bar, **T110-4** (§13.2): law 1 after every commit
across the family's fixtures, the state sweep, a randomised op sequence on fixed seeds, and every path, with its
structures unlocked, placement-locked and shape-locked (J147), and a skeleton's round trip (§13.2; J146, PRB7D-LCK-19).
That bar is what makes a missing example harmless.

### The worked examples under the laws (J146)

R7-02 – R7-07 and Part P (passes 1 and 2) stand as worked examples, each sentence and cell illustrating the law it cites
(R7-02's banner maps them). Seven corrections hold across every example, so that none contradicts the laws (the sixth
J147's, the first rewritten by J148 and the seventh J149's):
1. (J148) Every example that moves a standing wall's gate, postern or tower along its line for a meeting — law 2 (b)'s
   former moves, a gate's way following it, a fate or `reshaped` entry `gateMoved` or `towerMoved` — or that drops,
   refuses or waits a newer structure only because one stands in its way, reads by law 2: the gate, postern or tower
   stays (Tier 1); the newer bends itself (law 2 (a); an aqueduct passing over one its arches clear, law 1 (5)), else
   Tier-3 things move (b), else the op gives way (c). A gate, postern or tower the op itself lays or places (S7's
   towers, S5a's pin, a gate the user places or slides, one laid again on a run its own law re-traces) is law 2 (a)'s and stands
   (J146, PRB7D-LCK-01; J148; PRB7F-LCK-03); a run F-REDERIVE's rung widens to the next towers reads law 2 (c): never
   widened (law 1 (11); J146, PRB7F-TIM-01).
2. Every `refuse.locked` an example gives where a held building blocks a wall, a retaining wall's stroke or the raised
   way's own edit reads `refuse.raised.meeting` naming the building, with no Unlock (law 2 (c)).
3. Every ramp, stair or foot an example widens "by the band's half" — the pull's impassables, a lawful crossing, *Line
   and band* — is widened by the wall strips' width there (law 1 (4)); a road deck's support is kept off by nothing (law
   1 (3): laid through), an aqueduct's only where its channel runs above the wall's top (law 1 (2): over; J149; J146,
   PRB7F-GEN-03).
4. A citation, in an END line or a FIX, of the lock sentence, the bent-group sentence, the fixed-place sentence or
   (G1)–(G3) reads as a citation of §2.7's laws — law 2 (the lock sentence; its reach law 2's entry), law 2 (a) with law
   3 (the bent-group sentence), law 1 (6) with law 2 (the fixed-place sentence), law 2 (a)–(b)'s reach, law 4 and law 2 (c) ((G1), (G2), (G3); J148) — the sentence being its worked example.
5. Every "a fate" an example gives, by hand, to a move of the op's own structure — a part, crossing, end, gate or anchor
   of the user's stroke, or of a drawn wall its author's op changes — reads "part of its bend line" (law 4); a fate is
   given only to a thing held in placement (*reshaped*, law 4's (G2); J147), and every change of an unheld structure at a meeting — an
   unheld arch vault's chamber removed or laid again — is one of the op's consequences (§7.3 item 4), never a fate
   (J146, PRB7D-LCK-07).
6. Every example that holds a thing because it was drawn, dragged, declared, placed or pinned by its declaring event —
   "locked by its drawing", "declared, so held", a building held by its `placeAt`, a drawn wall "changed by its author's
   new declaration" — reads it as held only where the user locked it, on the axis the example names (placement where it
   names none); a thing drawn or declared and not locked meets as an unlocked one (law 1's terms; J147). Every example
   that says a lock or pin holds placement and never shape, gives a locked side's cell as its unheld cell's, or makes,
   caps, merges or leaves open at a meeting something it calls locked, pinned or held, reads it as placement-locked; no
   op makes or deepens a meeting with a shape-locked side (law 1 (10); J150), every list an example gives of lawful
   crossings, impassables or what a stroke keeps clear reading so (J146, PRB7E-LCK-08). §7.1–§7.4's and §5.3's declared
   row's own words (E-D1, E-D6, E-D8 – E-D10) stand until round 8 (Part A's hand-off).
7. (J149) Every example that keeps an aqueduct's supports off a wall's band (*the edge*, a support slid off the band,
   the band's half at a support's ring), bends a wall or a course so that a wall crosses an aqueduct under a span or
   through a `cleared` support's gap, caps a run beneath an aqueduct's span, keeps a fixed place out of an aqueduct's
   zone, or keeps a gate, postern or tower out of an aqueduct's strip or a course clear of one (J146, PRB7F-LCK-08),
   reads law 1 (2), (5) and (6): a wall and an aqueduct cross wherever the wall's line crosses the deck line, in the
   form the aqueduct's level there gives — over, its supports off the band and its spans clearing the wall, where its
   channel runs above the wall's top (such an example stands), else merged (J146, PRB7F-GEN-03) —, a gate, postern,
   tower or fixed place standing under the aqueduct only in an over crossing whose spans clear it, a gate's way laid
   after it passing by §4.3's crossing rule (PRB7G-GEN-04), never an anchor (law 1 (4); J146, PRB7F-LCK-06,
   PRB7F-GEN-08); a road deck's examples stand (J149: road decks unchanged).


### The family's veto list, restated (J146)

This list supersedes R7-02's list 1–22, which stays as the record. Each item is a behaviour a builder would see; each may
be vetoed by naming it; "law n" gives the law it comes from and [n] the old items it restates. Items that only follow from
another (bookkeeping, report counts) are dropped.
1. **(law 1 (1))** Nothing joins a raised way to the ground but its two ends: no gate, station or wall opening on a deck,
   a deck passing over a wall being no crossing of it. *Veto option:* R5-02's raised gate. [1]
2. **(law 1 (3); law 2 (a))** A road deck never crosses a wall that stood when that stretch of its course was laid
   (J146, PRB7D-LCK-09): it comes down before the wall, its road going on
   through a gate beyond the foot; one whose two ends are fixed is not built (at an epoch it waits), and a re-shape,
   warp or `revert` that would carry a standing deck newly across a wall is bent back, else refused, a crossing it
   keeps keeping its date (J146, PRB7F-LCK-04); a gate route at
   generation whose gate would fall on its deck is re-solved off it. [1, 17]
3. **(law 1 (3))** A wall laid after a road deck passes beneath it ungated, built through the deck's supports in its band.
   *Veto option:* a gate standing on the deck over the wall's line. [3]
4. **(law 1 (2), (4); J149 — the owner's approval, the forms' texts the program's)** Every aqueduct crosses every town,
   castle, village or quay wall at its own level, in either order, wherever the wall's line crosses its deck line: over
   the wall on an arch that clears its top where its channel runs above it — its supports off the band —, merged where
   its channel runs within the wall's height — the wall built up to and around the aqueduct's arch or pier,
   the water passing through it (the Porta Maggiore form: no gate, the aqueduct standing whatever becomes of the wall)
   (J146, PRB7F-GEN-03); nothing of either moves for it, and no wall runs along beneath a raised way or ends under it
   (PRB7F-LCK-05). *Veto option:* the
   merged form left out — an aqueduct whose arch cannot clear the wall bending its course to one that does, else not
   built (at an epoch waiting). [2, 8]
5. **(law 1 (4); new, J146)** No town, castle, village or quay wall, nor its setbacks, glacis or berm, lies over a raised
   way's ramp, stair or foot (a retaining wall may, the deck passing over it); the
   gate where a road comes down from a deck stands beyond the foot, clear of the ramp — measured by the glacis even where
   a moat runs, a berm never narrowing it, and a village's own palisade, earthwork or stone circuit by its 8.5 m band, a
   town ring handed down keeping its own (J146, PRB7D-GEN-04, PRB7D-TIM-04). *Veto option:* the wall's band alone
   kept off them, its setbacks allowed over a ramp. [3, 8]
6. **(law 1 (5); law 2 (a)–(c); J148 — the owner's rule and choice, the tier texts the program's)** No gate, postern or
   tower stands under a deck, standing or fallen, but under an aqueduct's arch that clears it and the wall, a gate's way
   laid after it passing square beneath (J149; J146, PRB7F-TIM-02, PRB7F-LCK-06; PRB7G-GEN-04), and none, once standing,
   is moved for a meeting: a raised way or a wall laid later plans round them by its own course or line — a gate or
   tower it lays itself placed clear, a gate the user slides going back toward where it came from —, else the soft
   things (a ground way, a plot, an unlocked building) move round them, else the later thing gives way (J146,
   PRB7D-LCK-01, PRB7D-GEN-02; J148). *Veto option:* none beyond the tier texts (J148 is the owner's rule). [13]
7. **(law 1 (6); law 2)** The places a wall's law fixes — a river's water gate, an anchor with its tower, a harbour-mouth
   chain, a castle's weld vertices, town-side gate and corner towers — never move along the wall and never stand under or
   beside a road deck, nor under an aqueduct whose arches do not clear them, an anchor, which ends its wall's line,
   under none (J149; law 1 (4); J146, PRB7F-GEN-08): a later wall moves its river
   crossing or anchor along the river or shore, a later chain to other heads, a later castle is sited elsewhere, a later
   raised way bends (an elevated road to a citadel ends on the castle street outside it); where none can, the later
   thing gives way. *Veto option:* a water gate lawful under a road deck's span whose soffit clears its gatehouse. [14]
8. **(law 1 (7))** A wall beneath a road deck is cut down to the lowest arch above it, never raised to it; beneath an
   aqueduct it keeps its own top, over or merged (law 1 (2); J149). [6]
9. **(law 1 (8))** No arch vault's chamber stands in a span while anything lies across it — a wall, an absorbed ring's run
   not yet dismantled, a retaining wall, a street, a moat, a marsh, another raised way laid before it, or anything laid
   there while it stood open (a square, a plot, another raised way's pier) —; it comes back when nothing does, at the end
   of that edit or epoch (at generation with the underground layer); its own chamber and what it seats never keep it
   open, and a ring converted to a road no longer counts as a wall (J146, PRB7D-TIM-01, PRB7D-TIM-03; PRB7E-GEN-01,
   PRB7E-TIM-01, PRB7E-TIM-02). *Veto options:* the chamber laid again at `absorbWall`'s commit [15]; each way re-routed
   off the span instead [18]; a retaining wall bent off vaulted spans [20]. [5, 15, 18, 20]
10. **(law 1 (9))** A building the user seated in an arch vault and locked, on either axis, is never disturbed by a wall
    laid while it is locked: the wall gives way (it crosses under another arch, else is refused or waits), and the
    raised way's own edit that would bring a wall across its span is bent or refused; a wall laid while it was unlocked
    stays when it is locked again (a lock's undo and redo round 8's, J150); one seated and not locked holds nothing, its
    chamber removed under a later wall as any chamber is, the building kept in existence as its kind's are (J147). [12]
11. **(law 2 — J144's rule, its reading the program's; J147; J148)** A placement lock or pin never refuses or skips a
    meeting of a wall and a raised way: the locked thing takes the soft readings there (a vault left open, a wall
    merged or capped) and a gate made by hand, and stays locked; only an op on the locked thing itself is refused; and drawing,
    dragging or declaring a thing holds nothing (J147). *Veto option:* UC18's locked vault kept with its chamber in the
    wall, the wall kept off its span instead. [9]
12. **(law 2 *Carried way*; law 2 (b), J148)** A raised road drawn by hand makes its road's gate in every wall it
    crosses on the ground, a placement-locked one shown as *reshaped*, the gate standing thereafter as any, a
    shape-locked one never — its road re-routed through the nearest gate of that wall a road may pass, else refused
    (J147; PRB7E-LCK-03; PRB7F-LCK-02, -07); a ring regenerated across a kept road deck's way gives it a gate where it
    crosses the way's ground course — at stage 1 beside the main gates, as a harbour gate is — else re-routes the way
    through one of its gates, unless the way is shape-locked (PRB7F-GEN-05; PRB7G-GEN-01), and a generated road deck's
    route is solved again with its stage (J146, PRB7D-GEN-07; PRB7E-GEN-05). *Veto option:* the gate refused on a
    placement-locked wall, the user unlocking first. [10]
13. **(law 2 (c))** Where no lawful shape exists the op gives way: by hand refused with the nearest lawful choices and
    *Show me why* (never an Unlock); at generation the newer thing not built or its wall stage left unwalled, reported;
    at an epoch it waits — a river shift that would leave a wall with no lawful shape waits too. *Veto options:* the
    existing fates and bends alone [11]; the river moved and the run left breached across it [22]. [11, 22]
14. **(law 3)** Each choice is the nearest lawful shape — the value an op set kept before it is bent (PRB7F-LCK-04) —,
    then a fixed order (the lower address, then the smaller value, then the place first by x,
    then y; J146, pass 5: law 3's key (3) in its own order): the same map every time, on every host (J146, PRB7D-LCK-11,
    PRB7D-GEN-08). [—]
15. **(law 4)** Every reshape of something the user placement-locked is shown — the fate *reshaped* by hand, a report
    entry otherwise — and undone by undo, a locked wall's top cut down under a deck and a locked part of the user's own
    structure among them; the user's own stroke, bent, shows as its bend, never as a fate, and a change of something
    nobody locked (an unlocked arch vault, a drawn wall) is listed among the edit's consequences (J146, PRB7D-LCK-06,
    PRB7D-LCK-07, PRB7D-LCK-08; J147). [11]
16. **(law 2 (c))** A frame growth's extension keeps the deck's course and its arches' state, and its old end span
    wherever a wall crosses it (PRB7G-TIM-02); past a bound with no fitting course it keeps a pier in the water or a
    tall pier, reported. *Veto option:* a merged span past the clear-span bound. [7, 16]
17. **(law 2 (a))** A hand repair of a fallen span rebuilds it on the way's line, over a wall laid since. *Veto option:*
    the repair refused where a later wall runs beneath. [21]
18. **(law 2 (a); J148)** A wall re-sectioned on its trace (a palisade rebuilt in stone, a stretch re-graded) keeps its
    trace, gates and towers where its new band is lawful, is re-traced locally where not, and waits or is refused where a
    standing gate's or tower's new footprint would meet a strip (no gate or tower moved). *Veto option:* the old section
    kept under the span. [19]
19. **(law 1; item 114)** A run beneath a deck decays, breaches and recovers by its own condition, the deck standing on
    its own supports; a wall's setback, glacis and berm are no obstacle to a support. [2, 4]
20. *(Withdrawn, J148: no gate moves for a meeting.)* [—]
21. **(law 4; new, J146, PRB7D-LCK-17)** An undo is exact or refused: an undo that would bring a structure back across what
    was built since is refused, naming the edits to undo first. *Veto option:* the undone structure bent clear (an
    inexact undo). [—]
22. **(law 2 *A warp*; new, J146, PRB7D-LCK-18; J148)** A warp is resolved as one stroke: what it carries into a meeting
    is reshaped as a drawn stroke's would be (a crossing moved along the deck), the gates and towers it carries moved no
    further for the meeting — the warp refused where one would stand unlawfully (law 1 (11); PRB7F-LCK-03) —, never
    halved for the meeting. *Veto option:* the warp's
    step halved until it makes no unlawful meeting (§3.6). [—]
23. **(law 1 (10); J147, the owner's rule, its reading the program's; J150)** A shape lock is honoured: no op makes or
    deepens a meeting with a thing whose shape the user locked — no gate made in it, no aqueduct merged into it, no wall
    across its arch vaults, no top cut lower —; the later thing bends itself, else gives way (by hand refused, at
    generation not built or the stage's ladder, at an epoch it waits); a reading it had follows what stands when the
    other side goes; its behaviour under an undo, a redo, a re-grade or a repair round 8's (J150). *Veto option:* a
    shape lock read as the outline alone (a trace or course and its band), a later wall still leaving its vault open.
    [—]

## Part F — THE FAMILY RULING

### R7-02 Raised ways and walls, closed by construction: the lock, the bent group, the fixed places and both tables complete

> (J146) **Worked examples, non-normative.** From J146 this ruling — its lock, bent-group and fixed-place sentences,
> *Strips, zones and nearest points*, the four outcomes, both tables, (M1)–(M6), the attack and the equivalence — is a
> set of worked examples of Part L's laws. It illustrates them so: the lock sentence law 2 (its reach law 2's *Entry*;
> (G1) law 2 (a)–(b)'s reach (J148); (G2) law 4; (G3) law 2 (c)); *Strips, zones and nearest points* law 1's terms and law 3; *over the band*
> law 1 (2), (5), (6) with law 2 (a)–(b); *down before the wall* law 1 (3) with law 2 (a) and *Carried way*; *beneath* law 1 (2)–(9) with law 2 (a)–(b); *unchanged
> meeting* law 4 (a re-check that finds nothing to resolve); the bent-group sentence law 2 (a) with law 3; the
> fixed-place sentence law 1 (6) with law 2; each column C1–C13 a state law 1 reads, C8 law 2's *Entry*; each row W1–W14
> law 4's re-check on that op's paths; the way-and-channel table §4.3's crossing rule with law 1 (1) and (8). Where any
> of it differs from Part L, Part L governs (Part L's corrections hold throughout — seven since J149); a case none of it lists is
> decided by the laws, and is no finding. The veto list below is superseded by Part L's restated list. Spots corrected
> under J146 are marked "(J146)".

**Governs (the family's cross-cutting findings; every other family finding is a cell or a sentence below, ruled in
R7-03 – R7-07):** the lock — VDC6-01, VEW-r6-04, VAL-R6-01 = VEW-r6-02, VAP-r6-01; the bent group — VEL6-r6-04, VAT-r6-02
= VV910-r6-01, VPC-r6-03, VCS-r6-01, VAT-r6-04; the fixed places — VM-R6T-01, VEW-r6-03; and the tables' cells —
VM-R6T-03, VAF-R6T-02, VAP-r6-02, VEL6-r6-02, VM-R6T-02, VV56-r6-03, VAL-R6-06, VV78-r6-01. **Home:** §2.7's support step
(6) (a)–(b) and (d) (model). **Supersedes** what R7-01 (5) names; R6-02 and its Part P stand otherwise, as amended here.

**Why this form.** Round 6 ruled one form whichever is laid first (R6-02), and the findings that remain are its edges: a
lock or pin that each unit read as refusing a different meeting (VDC6-01 at a hand aqueduct, VEW-r6-04 at an epoch's
founding, VAL-R6-01 = VEW-r6-02 at an arch vault, VAP-r6-01 at the hand rows); stretches the bent group never shaped
(along beneath, one side, ending at a pier, a carve's re-trace, a slid gate); places the wall's law fixes that the gate and
tower clause would have moved off (a water gate, an anchor, a weld); and cells naming tests that never set up their
state. The owner ruled the lock (J144); the round notes ask for two more sentences. So each family question now has one
home sentence or one cell, and the tables are written over all four axes.

**Owner rules checked.**
* **J144** (binding; the owner's words): "Locked regarding walls and aqueduct and similar structure should be considered
  for placement, not permanent shape. So when they do intersect, absolutely change the shape to be cohesive and coherent,
  despite being locked." The program's reading (J122, vetoable; PROGRESS's J144 row): the similar structures are the
  enceintes with their stretches, gates and towers, palisades, quay walls, retaining walls, and the raised ways with
  their supports, ramps, stairs, feet, spans and arch vaults; placement is where the thing is (a wall's trace and course, a
  raised way's course and deck line); shape is what changes at a meeting; a reshaped locked thing stays locked; item 101
  R13 reads, for these structures, as their placement held; buildings and other declared placements are not similar
  structures and stay held; and the three guardrails (G1) local, (G2) shown, (G3) the op creating the meeting gives way
  where no local shape exists, neither locked thing moved or broken. All kept, in the lock sentence's own words.
* **Item 110 (b)**: "a raised way crosses the ways beneath WITHOUT a junction"; "the spaces between piers stay open or hold
  the base's vaults"; the aqueduct "crossing the wall through an arch"; "the user may add or remove a raised way"; "a
  removal is an edit that holds through regeneration". Kept: no junction or gate on a deck; a `vault` span under a wall
  holds no vault while the wall stands (the space between piers then open, a wall in it); every aqueduct crosses every
  wall under one of its arches, in either order (the program's reading, J122, kept from R6-02); additions and removals
  untouched.
* **Item 110 (f)'s bars**: zero junctions; parts never share a planar arrangement with part 0; generated raised ways only
  at CITY and METROPOLIS with a reason, within the rung's cap; a removed one stays removed. Kept, each in T110-2 or T110-3.
* **Item 101**: R2 (a declaration bent to the nearest lawful one) — every hand bend below is R2's, and (G3)'s refusal
  offers the nearest lawful declarations; R13 (a declared or pinned thing is held) — as J144 reads it for these
  structures, their placement held, and unchanged for buildings.
* **Item 114** ("walls decay into gaps and patchwork when poor and safe, and recover"): every run decays and recovers by
  its own condition; no deck holds a wall and no wall a deck (rows W4, W5, W10, W12).
* Nothing else of the owner's text speaks to the meeting; the tables, the bent group, the fixed places, the state sweep
  and the vault timing at an absorb are program design under J122 (**vetoable**).

**What changes in behaviour, said plainly — the owner's veto list for the family, restated whole** (J146: superseded by Part L's restated veto list, 1–19, each item with its law; this list is kept as the record). R6-02's eight changes
stand as amended by its Part P; R7 adds 9–17, and its Part P 18–19 (pass 1) and 20–22 (pass 2) (each change Part P makes
to 9–17 marked there).
Each may be vetoed by naming it.
1. No crossing station, raised gate or wall opening anywhere; a priced road deck comes down before a standing trace, a road
   deck with fixed ends is not built across one (at an epoch it waits). *Veto option:* R5-02's raised gate.
2. An aqueduct created across a standing wall crosses it on a span, its supports off the band; the run beneath is not
   held and wears, breaches and recovers as every run.
3. A wall laid after a road deck passes beneath it ungated, a support in its band built into its line, never across its
   ramps, stairs or feet. *Veto option:* a gate standing on the deck over the wall's line.
4. The setback, glacis and berm are no obstacle to a support and are not stopped at the under-deck strip.
5. No arch vault stands in a `vault` span under a wall, in either order; its chamber is laid again once no wall lies
   there.
6. A run beneath a span keeps its own height, cut down to the lowest standing soffit there, never raised to it.
7. A frame growth's extension carries the old end span's `state` and hold and keeps the deck's course.
8. A wall laid after an aqueduct crosses it under one of its arches, never at a pier, and no wall crosses a ramp, stair or
   foot. *Veto option:* a later wall laid through an aqueduct's pier.
9. **(new, J144 — the owner's rule; its reading vetoable)** A lock, pin or declaration on a wall, a raised way or an arch
   vault never refuses or skips a meeting between them: an aqueduct drawn or founded across a drawn or locked wall
   crosses it on a span (no `refuse.locked`, no wait), and a locked arch vault under a wall laid after it keeps its span
   and lock but has no chamber while the wall stands; a founded course meets a held retaining wall or another held raised
   way as an unheld one (amended, PRB7-20). The lock reaches only meetings (amended, PRB7-03, PRB7-04): a hand op whose
   own target the user locked or pinned is still refused by §7.1's lock row, a drawn wall or declared raised way being
   changed by its author's new declaration; a carve or a moved river that would re-trace a run of a drawn or locked wall
   is refused as across any locked wall (amended, PRB7B-07); a landfall, decay or an epoch's conversion keeps every lock
   as before.
   *Veto option:* UC18's locked vault kept with its chamber in the band, the epoch's trace keeping off its under-deck
   strip instead (repair-r6-V04's option (b)).
10. **(new)** A road deck's carried way that comes down before a held wall makes its gate there by hand, the wall reshaped
    and kept locked (G2's fate *reshaped*); at generation, a regeneration and an epoch its route passes through a gate its
    path's own gate law gives, so no gate is made in a held wall but by hand (amended, PRB7-06). *Veto option:* the gate
    refused on a held wall (§7.1's lock row), the user unlocking first.
11. **(new)** The reshape of a held thing is always shown — a new edit fate, *reshaped*, which the user confirms, and a
    `reshaped` entry in the report's changes — and where no local shape exists the op is refused by a new key,
    `refuse.raised.meeting`, or off the hand path takes its ladder — at generation a stage's trace re-pulled round the
    raised way, else that stage left unwalled and reported (`W_RAISED_MEETING`), at an epoch waiting, at a village the
    palisade omitted (amended, PRB7-16) — each such ladder's raised-way rung running alone on its detection, every other
    rung keeping the crossing table's rules (amended, PRB7B-09). *Veto option:* the existing fates and bend lines alone.
12. **(new)** A wall gives way to a held building seated in an arch vault: the trace keeps its band and strips off the
    building's span, crossing the deck under another span — by hand moved there, a fate (amended, PRB7-01) — else waits or
    takes F-WALL's ladder, and by hand, with no other span, is refused with the building as the blocking entity; the
    raised way's own re-shape or re-spacing that would bring that span under a standing wall is bent clear, else refused
    naming the building (amended, PRB7B-05); a retaining wall drawn there gives way the same (amended, PRB7B-10).
13. **(new)** A hand or re-traced wall stretch is never left running along beneath a raised way, entering and leaving on
    one side, or ending under it: those parts move to the under-deck strip's edge, the wall's band kept outside it
    (amended, PRB7-08); a gate the user slides into the strip moves back to the edge it came from, one placed there to the
    nearer edge, its road following (amended, PRB7-10); a gate or tower is moved until its whole footprint is clear, and
    raised ways whose strips meet are kept clear of together, as one (amended, PRB7B-01, PRB7B-02). *Veto option:* such a
    drawing refused with its nearest lawful alternative.
14. **(new)** A river's water gate, an anchor's tower, a castle's weld vertices and its town-side gate are never moved
    along the wall for a raised way: a wall laid later crosses the river or anchors on its barrier at the nearest point
    clear of the raised way's fixed-place zone (amended, PRB7B-17), a castle sited later is sited clear of it — its gate
    on a clear side, else re-sited — never reshaped (amended, PRB7B-03), and a raised way laid later keeps its fixed-place
    zone clear of them (amended, PRB7-09); the strip and zone hold under a fallen span too, so no gate, tower or fixed place
    stands under a span that is later repaired (amended, PRB7-14). *Veto option:* a water gate lawful under a span whose
    soffit clears its gatehouse, capped like the run.
15. **(new)** An absorbed ring's arch vault returns when the last run of the old wall over it is dismantled, not when the
    wall is absorbed. *Veto option:* the vault laid again in `absorbWall`'s own commit, the standing run passing through
    its chamber's footprint.
16. **(new)** A frame growth's extension that meets a river or valley past a structural bound bends to the nearest course
    that fits, meeting every standing wall as a course laid then (amended, PRB7-15); with none, it keeps its course, each
    station slid off every obstacle but the water or height it fails, a pier standing in the water or past `maxPierM` kept
    and reported.
    *Veto option:* a merged span past `maxClearSpanM` kept instead of a pier in the water.
17. **(new)** At generation, a gate route whose first point on the stage-1 boundary lies on a viaduct is re-solved with no
    deck across that boundary, its gate on the ground; the gate routes' raised stretches then stand for the castle and the
    stage-1 trace, which are laid after them (amended, PRB7-02).
18. **(new, Part P — PRB7-13)** An arch vault comes back only once nothing crosses its span's strip: a way or channel that
    passed under the span while a wall stood over it (an intervallum, a street, a lane, the moat) keeps the span open after
    the wall goes, and the chamber is laid again when that way or water goes. *Veto option:* the chamber laid again when the
    wall goes, each such way re-routed off its strip and the channel stopped at its edges, as `wallToWay`'s ring road is.
19. **(new, Part P — PRB7-07)** A wall re-sectioned on its trace — a palisade rebuilt in stone, a stretch re-graded, its kind
    changed — keeps its trace where its new, wider band still crosses a raised way lawfully, and is re-traced locally by
    the bent-group sentence where it would not. *Veto option:* the old section kept under the span (the run narrowed
    there), its trace unchanged.
20. **(new, Part P pass 2 — PRB7B-10)** A retaining wall's line across a `vault` span's strip keeps that span open — no
    arch vault across its step — in either order, the chamber laid again when the line goes; a retaining wall drawn
    across a span whose chamber seats a held building moves to cross under another span or at a support, else is refused
    naming the building. *Veto option:* the drawn stroke bent off every `vault` span to cross under an `open` span or at a
    support, the chamber kept.
21. **(new, Part P pass 2 — PRB7B-06)** A hand repair of a dismantled span or a cleared support raises the new part on
    the way's own line, as a frame growth's extension keeps the deck's course: a span over a wall laid after the way
    stands over it again, its new supports slid off the band, refused only where no support can stand off the band.
    *Veto option:* the repair refused where a wall laid after the way runs beneath (the span left fallen).
22. **(new, Part P pass 2 — PRB7B-08)** An epoch's river shift, avulsion or dug canal that would re-trace a wall run
    with no lawful shape at a raised way — even with the run widened once to the next towers — waits, the river, the
    wall and the raised way unchanged, counted in `walls.waited`. *Veto option:* the river moved and the run left
    `breached` across it with no water gate, reported.

#### The texts §2.7 carries — written by the model in these words

(J146) The model writes Part L's laws first, as (6)'s normative bullet (Part H · model, E-M1), and the blocks below
after them under the heading **Worked examples** — every block below non-normative, each spot citing the law R7-02's
banner maps it to.

below, and conforms the sentences after them as (M1)–(M6) say.

**Block 1 — (6) (d), the crossing table, whole:**

  * (d) *a wall and a raised way* meet by this table, **the crossing table**, whichever was laid first, on every path —
    generation, a regeneration, an epoch, a village derivation and a hand edit — and with either side held or neither.
    Its lock sentence, its four outcomes, its bent-group sentence and its fixed-place sentence are defined once here and
    every other text cites them:

    considered for placement, not permanent shape"). The **similar structures** are walls of every kind — an enceinte with
    its stretches, gates and towers, a palisade, a quay wall, a retaining wall — and the raised ways — a viaduct, an
    elevated road, an aqueduct — with their supports, ramps, stairs, feet, spans and arch vaults. A lock, a pin or a
    declaration (§7.2; item 101 R13) holds a similar structure's **placement** — a wall's trace and course, a raised way's
    course and deck line, an arch vault's span — and never its **shape** where two of them meet: each change this table
    makes at a meeting — a gate or tower moved along, a stretch bent locally, a support slid off a band, a `vault` span
    read `open` with no chamber while a wall stands over it and its chamber laid again once none does, a gate made by hand
    where a road deck's carried way crosses a wall (*down before the wall*) (amended, PRB7-06) — is made whether either
    side is locked, pinned, declared or neither, and what
    it reshapes stays locked, pinned or declared (a locked arch vault's lock kept with its key, so the chamber V04 lays
    again carries it). No meeting of two similar structures is refused, skipped or altered for a lock: the lock axis is one

    (J146: shortened, the law saying it): whether an event may act on its own target at all is settled before any
    meeting — by hand a target the user locked or pinned (T10 `hold`) refused by §7.1's lock row, nothing written; a drawn
    wall or a declared raised way changed by its author's new declaration (§7.2 *Declared means held*), its event then
    taking its cell; a carve or a moved river that would re-trace a run of a drawn or locked wall refused as across any
    locked wall (`refuse.water`; §4.11 *Other crossings*), so the bent group's carved or river-moved run is always neither
    drawn nor locked; by the engine or a hazard a held target kept as §7.2 *Protection*, V06 §3.1 and V05 §3.16 (2) keep
    it, the ruin lapping round it. Once the event acts, the meeting it makes takes its cell. **The three guards** are

    neither held thing moved or broken. Buildings and every
    other declared placement are no similar structure and stay held as §7.2 holds them; a wall gives way to one
    (*beneath*, *A held building in an arch vault*). A **retaining wall** has no strip, gate or closure, so no row of this table reads it — its band is its line and its decorative towers' footprints are kept out of a strip as any tower's, moved along its line (§2.7's laws: law 1's terms, law 1 (5), law 3; J146) —, and *beneath* reads its line once: a `vault` span whose under-deck strip it crosses is
