Every PRB7-* and PRB7B-* amendment was read against Part L. All stand as worked examples, each illustrating the law
R7-02's banner maps its spot to, **save six, superseded in part** (each spot so corrected marked "(J146)"; the rest of
each amendment stands):

| id | the part superseded | by |
|---|---|---|
| PRB7-01 | the held building's refusal by §7.1's lock row, `refuse.locked` naming the building (it offers an Unlock that releases nothing) | law 2 (c): `refuse.raised.meeting` naming the building, no Unlock |
| PRB7-08 | *Line and band*'s and the pulled impassables' keeping of a line off a ramp, stair or foot by the band's half alone | law 1 (4): the line, band and wall strips off the ends (veto item 5) |
| PRB7B-03 | "a tower at a castle's bailey vertex other than a weld vertex is a tower as any, moved along the bailey's curtain" | law 1's fixed places: a castle's vertex towers are tied to its claim |
| PRB7B-05 | the raised way's own edit refused by `refuse.locked` naming the building; "no other op moves a span" | law 2 (c); law 4 (width, class, `split`, `merge`, `warp` and `revert` give way the same) |
| PRB7B-10 | the retaining stroke's refusal by `refuse.locked` naming the building | law 2 (c) |
| PRB7B-12 | E-P9's and E-V6-16's ramps, stairs and feet widened by the band's half | law 1 (4): by the wall strips' width there (Part L's correction 3) |

Beyond the amendments, three of R7-02's own spots contradicted the laws and are corrected in place: W1·C8's "(nothing is
held at a first generation)" (law 2 *Newer*), the C9 cells' "(V07 runs no pathway)" (law 4), and every "a fate" given to a
move of the op's own stroke (law 4; Part L's correction 5). Checked and standing: the lock sentence's reach (PRB7-03,
PRB7-04, PRB7B-07) is law 2's *Entry*; *Strips, zones and nearest points* (PRB7-10, PRB7-14, PRB7B-01, PRB7B-02) is law 1's
terms and law 3; the gate by the path's gate law (PRB7-06, PRB7B-04) is law 2 *Carried way*; the `vault` reading cited by every
reader (PRB7-13, PRB7-25, PRB7B-10, PRB7B-11) is law 1 (8); the ladders' raised-way rungs (PRB7-16, PRB7B-08, PRB7B-09)
and the frame growth past a bound (PRB7-15) are law 2 (c); the hand repair keeping its course (PRB7B-06) is law 1 (3)'s
course date; PRB7-19's REJECT stands.

**No major can be raised against an example.** Under J146 an example that differs from the laws, or a case no example
lists, is at most a minor (the example corrected to the laws); a major in this family names a case where Part L gives two
outcomes or none, an owner rule Part L crosses, or an invariant of law 1 that neither §5.3's row nor T110-4 checks.

## Part P — Pass 4: the laws probe (J146)

`coherence/adjudicate-r7-laws2` (2026-10-02) decides probe pass 4 — the laws probe, three lenses that attacked Part L
itself: `probe-r7d-time.md` (10: PRB7D-TIM-01 – 10), `probe-r7d-locks.md` (20: PRB7D-LCK-01 – 20) and
`probe-r7d-gen.md` (11: PRB7D-GEN-01 – 11); 41 findings, 2 blockers that are one defect (PRB7D-TIM-01 = PRB7D-GEN-01),
29 major, 10 minor. The findings are no longer missing ops but terms defined twice or not at all, law 2's order, law 3's
units and ties, what the check can see, and timing; each is closed by refining Part L **by class** — one definition per
term, one order, one total tie-break, stored order fields, one definition of a commit — never by adding cases, and Part L
is amended in place, each change marked "(J146, PRB7D-…)". **CLOSED BY LAW n** where the laws as written decide the case
uniquely; **LAW REFINED** where Part L now carries the refinement quoted; **OWNER-RULE CLASH** where a law crossed an
owner rule; **REJECTED** with the reason. No owner rule is changed, and every reading of the owner's words stays the
program's (J122, vetoable).

**Owner-rule clashes (flagged at the head, as J146's grading asks): one — PRB7D-LCK-04.** Law 2 (b)'s "its gates (each
gate's way following it) … move along its line" moved a gate whose way the user drew, locked or pinned, and so re-routed
a held ground way — a declared placement, no similar structure — at a meeting it did not name: against item 101 R13
("declared means held") as the program's reading of J144 applies it ("Buildings and other declared placements are not
'similar structures' and stay held as before" — PROGRESS J144, the program's reading, J122). The narrowest change that
restores the rule: law 2 (b) never moves a gate whose way is held, passing it over as it passes a fixed place, and law 1
(10) keeps every held thing that is no wall-like structure or raised way whole; the behaviour is new veto 20. Two more
findings touched an owner rule and are decided in its favour, so no clash remains in the refined laws: PRB7D-TIM-02's
reading A (an aqueduct founded across an absorbed ring standing a pier on a `breached` run, against item 110 (b)'s
"crossing the wall through an arch" as the program reads it) is excluded by *What stands of a wall*; and PRB7D-LCK-01 /
PRB7D-GEN-02's inversion (correction 1 and veto 6 running (b) before (a)) is undone, law 2's order being the one the
method the owner chose states (PROGRESS J146's METHOD, "the op creating or changing the meeting reshapes its own
structure first; failing that, the standing structure's local shape only" — the method's words the program's, the
choice the owner's). (Superseded, J147: the owner's later rule — drawing or dragging holds nothing, and a thing is held
only on the axis the user locks — removes this clash's ground; PRB7D-LCK-04 is decided again under it, and *J147
applied*, below, records the change.)

**Totals.** 41 decided: **10 CLOSED BY LAW** (the ten minors — law 1: 4; law 2: 4; law 3: 1; law 4: 1), **30 LAW
REFINED** (both blockers and 28 majors), **1 OWNER-RULE CLASH** (PRB7D-LCK-04), **0 REJECTED**. Nine minors' examples or
END texts corrected to the laws (all but PRB7D-TIM-05, which the refined law 1 (3) closes with no example changed).
Part L: 4,209 words before, 6,200 after (its markers included). The veto list runs 1–22: items 2, 5, 6, 9, 12, 14 and 15
amended, 20 – 22 new.

**How this pass closes by class.** *One definition per term*, each a function of what stands: a **commit** per path
(law 1 holding at its end, every provider inside it), **what stands of a wall** (a `cleared` record by its cause),
**the band** by the derivation that laid it, **the wall strips' width** with the glacis whatever the moat and the wider
of two walls where they meet, **a span's strip** and a **standing span**, **anything across a strip** with **open
ground**, **held** with **explicit hold**, **shape** with the cap, and **order** with its two stored fields. *One order*:
law 2 (a), then (b), then (c), for every op on every path, (b) acting on the shape (a) would take with the standing
structure's gates, posterns and towers passable, correction 1 and veto 6 agreeing. *One total order*: law 3's three keys
— what changes, least displacement, a canonical key (address, value, then x and y) no two shapes share — after which no
tie can be raised. *The check reads what the commit leaves and its event*: stored order where lines cross, the commit's
undo record for what is held. *Once each*: a hand op with no lawful shape refused (law 2 (c)), an undo exact or refused
as dependent (law 4), a warp one op whose result law 2 resolves (law 2 *A warp*).

| id | sev | decision | how the laws decide it | text changed (J146) |
|---|---|---|---|---|

### Passes 1–3 re-checked against the refined laws (J146, pass 4)

Every Part P row of passes 1–3 was read against Part L as refined. All stand as worked examples **save seven, superseded
in part** (each spot so corrected marked "(J146, PRB7D-…)"; the rest of each row stands):

| id | the part superseded | by |
|---|---|---|
| PRB7-10 | the tie "to the one nearer the line's first vertex in its vertex order" | law 3's canonical key (PRB7D-GEN-08, PRB7D-LCK-12) |
| PRB7-29 | an unheld chamber laid again "a fate by hand" | law 4's (G2): one of the op's consequences (PRB7D-LCK-07) |
| PRB7B-01 | (i)'s nearest lawful crossing "along the deck line the part first met" | law 3's key (2), the largest move first (PRB7D-LCK-13) |
| PRB7C-LCK-06 | law 3's "the op takes law 2 (c)" for a bend with no effect | law 3's last sentence: (b), then (c) (PRB7D-LCK-03) |
| PRB7C-LCK-10 | every skeleton's round trip reproducing its source's digest | the check: only where no declaration met a generated structure laid before it (PRB7D-LCK-19) |
| PRB7C-TIM-06 | a village kind's band by kind, `palisade.bandM` each side of the section | *the band* by its derivation, `palisade.bandM` from the line (PRB7D-TIM-04) |
| PRB7C-GEN-12 | the check "at every stage end and every commit" read per batch | the check at the end of every commit as *Commit* defines it (PRB7D-TIM-01) |

Checked and standing: PRB7C-TIM-09 (a burial clears its record's runs, so the chamber is laid again) now holds by *What
stands of a wall*; PRB7C-LCK-04 (a warp's result a given shape) holds with E-D17; PRB7-15's bent extension and PRB7B-06's
repair keeping its course are *Order*'s; PRB7-01's widened strip is law 1 (9)'s margin for a wall line laid after the
seat; PRB7C-GEN-05's widened trigger is completed by R7-03 (a)'s exclusion (PRB7D-GEN-09); PRB7-19's REJECT stands.

**Verdict.** With these refinements the laws are complete and determinate for every case the three lenses ran: every
term the laws use is defined once as a function of what stands or of a stored field (a commit, what stands of a wall, the
band and the wall strips' width, a span's strip, standing, anything across, held, shape, order), law 2 resolves every
meeting in one order on every path, law 3 ends in a key no two shapes share, and every clause of law 1 is computable at
a commit's end from what it leaves and its event — so the classes the pass raised close at once: no term can split again
over a record's cause, a product change, a moat's timing or a shared place; no example can reverse law 2's order; no tie
can be left open, on a closed line, a terrain edge, a value or a course; no clause of law 1 needs a date or a "before"
that nothing stores; and no clause binds before its provider has run. The one owner-rule clash (a held way dragged by a
moved gate) is restored by the narrowest change and flagged. A further probe should attack the refinements themselves:
*Order*'s writers and keepers op by op (a merge of stretches of different dates, an epoch's re-anchored stretch, an
import, an upgrade writing the fields), whether law 3's key (1) orders every value edit and every given shape as the
examples expect, (b)'s shape for a search whose standing structure has no movable part, the *commit* boundary on a
chained hand edit that runs a stage's operator and on a village epoch, *anything across a strip* against every reader of
a span's `use` (V09, V10, §11.1), and whether the check's reading of the undo record holds on every path that keeps one.

### J147 applied (2026-10-02)

`coherence/adjudicate-r7-j147` applies the owner's rule J147 (PROGRESS J147, read whole) to Part L. **The owner's rule:**
drawing or dragging a thing holds nothing — it moves with the settlement, and to pin a thing the user drags it and locks
it; locks have two independent axes, a **placement** lock (where a thing is; its shape still evolves) and a **shape**
lock (its shape and plot size — outline and size only); where a raised way meets a wall with a shape lock, the shape
lock is honoured — the shape-locked wall never changes, the newer thing bends itself, else gives way — while a
placement-locked wall still reshapes at a meeting (J144). **The program's reading and sequencing** (J122, vetoable, not
the owner's words): *held* means locked, on the axis named, and nothing else; J144's rule is the placement lock's for
every lockable thing; a shape-locked structure is passed over by law 2 (b) as a fixed place is; the family's laws adopt
this now, and the two-axis lock itself and every reader outside the family's meetings take it in round 8 (Part A's
hand-off, which also says that no family check reports §7.2's text against Part L before then). Applied by class: one
definition of *held* per axis, every clause that held a thing because it was drawn, declared, pinned by its declaring
event or placed removed, and every law that read *held* re-run against it.

**Clauses changed in Part L (12), each marked "J147" there:**
1. *Held* (terms): "**Held** is locked, on the axis named, and nothing else (J147): **held in placement** is
   placement-locked — the lock the user makes (T10 `hold`, UC18), a pin among them — and holds where a thing is, its
   shape still changing at a meeting (J144); **held in shape** is shape-locked and holds its shape and size … A thing
   drawn, dragged, declared or placed by hand holds nothing unless locked: it keeps its existence and meets as an
   unlocked thing of its kind." — replacing §7.2's locked, pinned or declared and the *explicit hold* term.
2. *A held building's strip* (terms): "a held building (one seated there, V04 §3.5.4 (d), and locked on either axis —
   J147)" — replacing "(a declared placement …)".
3. Law 1 (9): "no wall's line laid after the lock (*Order*, the lock's order its event's) lies in its held building's
   strip" — replacing "after the seat".
4. Law 1 (10): "a thing held in placement keeps its placement and a thing held in shape its shape, changed by no op but
   one that names it — a meeting changing a placement-held thing's shape alone (J144), a shape-held thing's not at all
   (J147)" — replacing the held structure's placement and the non-similar held thing kept whole.
5. Law 2 *Entry*: "a target the user locked refused, `refuse.locked`, as that row refuses one — its two axes round 8's
   (Part A); a target drawn, dragged, declared or placed by hand and not locked changed as an unlocked one is … No
   placement lock or pin … refuses, skips or alters a meeting (J144 …); a shape lock is honoured, its holder's shape
   changed by no meeting (law 1 (10); J147)" — replacing the explicit-hold and author's-declaration clauses.
6. Law 2 *Newer*: "at a regeneration every held structure — locked on either axis (J147) — is older than every stage".
7. Law 2 (b): "a gate's way re-routing locally as a shape change of that way (shown by law 4's (G2) where the way is
   placement-locked). Never a fixed place, a support, a held building, or anything shape-locked — a shape-locked
   structure or part, or a gate whose way is shape-locked —, which (b) passes over as it passes a fixed place" —
   replacing "a gate whose way is held" (PRB7D-LCK-04).
8. Law 2 *Carried way*: "a placement-locked one too (a shape change of that wall, shown by law 4's (G2)), never in a
   shape-locked one, where the declared way is bent by law 2 (a) to cross through a gate that wall has, else the stroke
   takes law 2 (c)", and "a locked road deck's at a regeneration" for "a held road deck's".
9. Law 4's (G2): "Every change a meeting makes to the shape of a thing held in placement — a placement-locked structure,
   part or way other than the op's own target, or a placement-locked part of the op's own (an arch vault, a gate, a
   tower) — is shown …; a thing held in shape no meeting changes (law 1 (10); J147)" — replacing "something held" and
   "under an explicit hold".

### J148 applied (2026-10-02)

**The owner's rule** (PROGRESS J148): a gate does not move — everything else revolves around it; where a meeting would
need a hard structure (a gate, an aqueduct or the like) to move, the easier of the two to move is the one moved, or the
conflict is avoided before it happens; the owner chose the program's three tiers (Tier 1, never moved by a meeting:
gates, posterns, towers and the fixed places; Tier 2, hard: wall runs and raised ways, the newer bending locally where
they meet; Tier 3, soft: ground ways, plots and unlocked buildings, which move round the hard ones; every new thing
first planning round the hard ones). **The program's** (J122, vetoable): the tier texts, and the reading that law 2
(b)'s moves of a standing wall's gates, posterns and towers are withdrawn, a meeting the newer's own local shape or a
Tier-3 move cannot resolve taking law 2 (c), the soft readings kept, J144's rule (a lock refuses no meeting) and J147's
axes standing. Applied by class: law 2's order is the tiers' order; every clause that moved a standing Tier-1 thing, and
what only that move needed, is withdrawn; nothing else of J144 or J147 changes — J147's "a placement-locked wall still
reshapes at a meeting" now means its soft readings only.

### J149 applied (2026-10-02)

**The owner's approval** (PROGRESS J149): an aqueduct crosses a wall at its own level, in one of three forms — **over**
(its channel above the wall's top, on an arch that clears the wall, a gate, postern or tower whose top it clears standing
under the arch), **merged** (its channel within the wall's height, the wall built up to and around the aqueduct's own arch
or pier, the water passing through it — the Porta Maggiore form) and **beneath** (a conduit under the wall); nothing hard
moves (J148); viaducts and elevated roads are unchanged; "then yes do those if they help". **The program's** (J122,
vetoable): the forms' texts; applying "if they help" — a form written where it removes more cases than it adds; and the
reading that the merged arch is the aqueduct's own masonry (item 114), no gate and no junction (item 110 (f)), never on a
gate's passage or in a tower, never into a shape-locked wall (J147), and taken by a wall laid later as by an aqueduct.

**The forms written: over and merged; beneath left out.**
* **Over** is written because it removes cases and adds none: a gate, postern or tower — Tier 1 since J148 — may stand
  under an arch that clears its top, so an aqueduct meeting a ring's gate or tower need not bend off it or give way (the
  commonest hand aqueduct gesture, PRB7E-LCK-01's case (2)), and S7 need not keep a gate out of an aqueduct's strip where
  its arches clear it; the form is read from what stands (the standing spans' soffits against the wall's top), nothing
  stored.
* **Merged** is written because, with over, it makes every crossing of an aqueduct's deck line lawful, so no op bends,
  slides, waits or is refused for one: it removes every clause that kept an aqueduct's supports off a band and every
  case those clauses made — a support slid off the band, *the edge*, a pulled trace treating supports as impassable, a
  wall bent to pass under an arch, the gap through a `cleared` support as a crossing's exception, an aqueduct refused or
  not built where no course keeps its supports off a band, and the cap beneath an aqueduct's span. It adds one soft
  reading (*The soft readings*) and two exclusions law 1 (5) and (10) already give (no merge on a gate's passage or in a
  tower; none into a shape-locked wall after its lock). (Superseded in part, J146, PRB7F-GEN-03, PRB7F-TIM-02: the
  owner's rule gives merged only to a channel within the wall's height, so an aqueduct whose channel runs above the
  wall's top crosses over only — its supports off the band, its spans clearing the wall —, the newer bending else
  giving way; and law 1 (5) keeps a gate, postern or tower out of a merged crossing by reading the wall's top too.)
* **Beneath** is left out because it would add cases and remove none: an aqueduct whose channel runs under the ground
  where it meets a wall is no raised way there — its buried stretch "stays a line on ground part 0" (§2.4's `aqueduct`
  class), its reach V04's foundations' (`conduitClearCm`) — so it makes no meeting of this family and the laws have
  nothing to decide; writing it would bring a buried stretch, its descent from the arcade and its foundation reach into
  the family as new cases, while every level of a raised channel at a wall is already above the wall's top (over) or
  within its height (merged). An aqueduct that goes underground before a wall meets it only by its ends (law 1 (4));
  what its buried stretch meets under the wall is the base's and V04's, never a meeting these laws decide.

**Clauses changed in Part L (10), each marked "J149" there:**
1. The lead: "(J149) The owner's J149 (PROGRESS J149: an aqueduct crosses a wall at its own level) is law 1 (2), (5) and
   (6): the forms **over** and **merged** are written, **beneath** is left out …".
2. Law 1 (2): "an enclosing wall's line meets an aqueduct's strip only to cross its deck line — entering on one long
   side, leaving by the other, meeting the deck line once there — and crosses it at the aqueduct's level, in one of two
   forms read from what stands, nothing of either record written (J149): **over**, where no standing support stands in
   the band there and every standing span above the band clears the wall's top, the band passing beneath the arch; else
   **merged**, the band built up to and around the aqueduct's arch or pier there at the wall's own top, the water passing
   through it in the aqueduct's channel — no gate (no way passes it), each keeping its own condition (item 114) —; so
   every aqueduct crosses every wall through an arch, over it or within it" — replacing "an enclosing wall's line meets
   an aqueduct's deck line only under a span or through a `cleared` support's ring, and its band meets a standing
   aqueduct support's ring at most along the ring's boundary".
3. Law 1 (5): "… under a standing span or a fallen one, but under an aqueduct whose every span over it clears its top,
   never on a support's ring (law 1 (2)'s over; J149)".
4. Law 1 (6): "no fixed place, with its footprint, lies in a road deck's zone, nor in an aqueduct's strip but where law 1
   (5) admits a gate or tower there (J149)" — replacing "lies in a zone".
5. Law 1 (7): "beneath a standing span of a road deck … ; beneath an aqueduct the wall keeps its own top (law 1 (2);
   J149)" — replacing "beneath a standing span".
6. *The zone*: "**The zone** of a road deck … ; an aqueduct has none (law 1 (6); J149)" — replacing "**The zone** of a
   raised way".
7. *The soft readings*: "a wall merged round an aqueduct's arch or pier (law 1 (2))" among them.
8. Law 1 (10): "no such aqueduct merged into it" — a shape-held wall merged into by no aqueduct laid or moved after its
   lock (J147 with J149).
9. Correction 3 (narrowed): "a support is kept off by nothing (law 1 (2), (3): a road deck's laid through, an
   aqueduct's merged; J149)" — replacing "a support still by the band's half (law 1 (2))".
10. Correction 7 (new): "(J149) Every example that keeps an aqueduct's supports off a wall's band (*the edge*, a support
    slid off the band, the band's half at a support's ring), bends a wall or a course so that a wall crosses an aqueduct
    under a span or through a `cleared` support's gap, caps a run beneath an aqueduct's span, or keeps a fixed place out
    of an aqueduct's zone, reads law 1 (2), (5) and (6) …; a road deck's examples stand (J149: road decks unchanged)."

**Withdrawn or narrowed to what remains** — every clause that existed only for the old aqueduct crossing: a support slid
off the band (kept off nothing; correction 3), a wall bent to pass under an arch (it crosses wherever its line crosses
the deck line once), a wall's top capped beneath an aqueduct's span (law 1 (7) caps beneath a road deck only), the gap
through a `cleared` support as a lawful crossing's exception (a `cleared` ring stays open ground, the crossing there
over), and an aqueduct's fixed-place zone (none); a gate, postern or tower admitted under an aqueduct's arch that clears
it (law 1 (5)). Examples read by correction 7; END lines by Part H's head, E-P1, E-P9, E-D2 and E-D3 rewritten.

PRB7B-02, PRB7B-03, PRB7B-06, PRB7B-12 and PRB7D-TIM-06.

**Veto items (J149):** **4** rewritten — "Every aqueduct crosses every town, castle, village or quay wall at its own
level, in either order, wherever the wall's line crosses its deck line: over the wall on an arch that clears its top,
else merged — the wall built up to and around the aqueduct's arch or pier, the water passing through it (the Porta

### The pass-5 decisions (J146)

**How this pass closes by class.** *One defect, three lenses:* *anything across a strip* never counts a raised way's own
reservation, its arch vault or what the chamber seats, and counts another raised way's whole strip only across a span
whose deck was fixed after it — so law 1 (8) has one fixed point for every span, whatever stands on it or crosses over it
(PRB7E-GEN-01, -LCK-06, -TIM-02). *Terms by what stands, once each:* what stands of a wall run by run (a conversion, a
burial or a delete removing runs, an absorbed record's runs until dismantled); the band by the construction that laid it
(VS3's wherever it runs); beyond the foot a region with its first point; the zone off the wall's line; placement and
shape as record fields. *One order, dated where it is fixed:* *Order* by the step that fixes a line, each stage's S5 steps
its own, and *Newer* at a regeneration for every structure it keeps where it stands. *Law 2 by tiers:* (a)'s reach a
bound that admits local re-lays, (b)'s passable set the Tier-3 things it may move and each moved within its own reach,
(c)'s rung with a reach of its own. *No date or frozen reading the commit cannot see:* law 1 (9) and (10) read only the
lines a commit lays or moves and the holds at its end, the readings from before following what stands; the check queries
every span a commit changes the ground under and compares record fields.

Law 1 (8) has one fixed point for every `vault` span — its own reservation and seat follow its reading —, and §5.3's row reads it so. Checked and standing: PRB7C-TIM-09 (a burial's chamber laid again) now holds run by run with E-V6-20; PRB7C-TIM-08 (a
chain laid after a raised way along its tie) is law 2 (a)'s — the op lays it; PRB7-10's and W13's slid gate back to its
edge is the op's own (law 2 (a), law 3's drag clause); PRB7-11 (a trace through a `cleared` ring) stands, the crossing
there over; PRB7-06 and PRB7C-LCK-12 (a gate made by hand in a placement-locked wall, never in a shape-locked one) stand
as J147 left them, the gate a soft reading (J148); PRB7-15's bent extension and PRB7B-06's repair keeping its course are
*Order*'s; PRB7-16, PRB7B-08 and PRB7B-09's rungs stand with law 2 (c)'s reach; PRB7D-TIM-01's commit stands, its vault
reading now *The soft readings*; PRB7-19's REJECT stands.

**Verdict.** With J148, J149 and these refinements the laws are complete and determinate for every case the three lenses
ran. Every term is defined once, as a function of what stands — read run by run where a record holds runs — or of a
stored field: what stands of a wall, the band by its construction, beyond the foot with its first point, the zone on and
off the line, anything across a strip with its exclusions and its order between raised ways, placement and shape as
record fields, order by the step that fixes a line; law 2 resolves every meeting in the tiers' order — the newer
planned round the hard ones within a bounded reach that admits local re-lays, then only Tier-3 things passable and
moved, each within its own reach, then the op giving way, its generation rung with a reach of its own; law 1 (9) and (10)
read only the lines a commit lays or moves and the holds at its end, so no lock order or frozen reading is needed; and
the check queries every span whose ground a commit changes and compares record fields. The classes the pass raised close
at once: no span's reading can turn on itself or on two raised ways' order; no term can split over a record's existence,
the site a construction runs at, or a point read as a region; no meeting moves a gate, postern, tower or fixed place
(J148), so nothing that follows a moved gate — its way, its moat deck, its fate or entry, a passed-over set — can be
raised again; no aqueduct crossing has a case beyond its level (J149: over or merged, read from what stands); a lock made
live again by an undo or a redo cannot split law 1 (9); and a shape lock cannot hold a reading up against a fall, a
removal or a repair. Of J149's forms, over and merged were written, each removing cases and adding none the laws did not
already give; beneath was left out, a buried channel being no raised way at the wall (§2.4) and its form adding cases
without removing any. A further probe should attack the new texts themselves: *the tiers* against every op that lays a
Tier-1 thing of its own (S7's towers by spacing after a re-trace, a re-section's re-derived gatehouse, a castle re-sited,
a warp) — whether "laid" and "moved" split there, and whether any bar observes a meeting that moves an unheld Tier-1
thing (law 1 (10) sees it only for a held one); law 1 (2)'s over and merged at a fallen or `ruined` span, a support
straddling the band's edge, a gatehouse taller than the wall and a run that decays beneath a merged arch; law 2 (b)'s
Tier-3 moves (a plot re-cut, an unlocked building moved) at an epoch and at a village, and how they are reported; the
region beyond the foot against S4's and S5b's gate choice; *Order*'s steps inside an epoch's new walled stage and a
village derivation; and *anything across a strip*'s order between raised ways where three decks cross or a re-shape
re-dates a deck.

## Part P — Pass 6: the laws probe (J146)

`coherence/adjudicate-r7-laws4` (2026-10-02) decides probe pass 6 — the laws probe's third pass, three lenses that
attacked Part L as J148, J149 and pass 5 left it: `probe-r7f-time.md` (4: PRB7F-TIM-01 – 04), `probe-r7f-locks.md` (10:
PRB7F-LCK-01 – 10) and `probe-r7f-gen.md` (9: PRB7F-GEN-01 – 09); 23 findings, 0 blockers, 15 major, 8 minor (majors per
pass: 31, 29, 19, 15). Two pairs are one defect, each closed once: PRB7F-LCK-08 with PRB7F-GEN-06 (correction 7's
list), and PRB7F-TIM-03 with PRB7F-LCK-03's proposed law 1 (11) (no bar sees a Tier-1 thing moved). Part L is amended in
place by class — one term, one order, one clause each —, never by adding cases, each change marked "(J146, PRB7F-…)":
**CLOSED BY LAW n** where the laws as written decide the case uniquely; **LAW REFINED** where Part L now carries the
refinement quoted; **OWNER-RULE CLASH** where a law crossed an owner rule, ruled by the narrowest change and flagged
here; **REJECTED** with the reason. No owner rule is changed, and every reading of the owner's words stays the
program's (J122, vetoable).

**Owner-rule clashes (flagged at the head, as J146's grading asks): one — PRB7F-GEN-03.** J149's rule, the owner's
approval of the program's proposal (PROGRESS J149), names the forms by the aqueduct's level: OVER, "its channel above
the wall's top, it passes on an arch that clears the wall"; MERGED, "its channel within the wall's height, the wall is
built up to and around the aqueduct's own arch or pier" — the proposal it answered saying the level "picks the form".
Law 1 (2) as pass 5 wrote it read the form from where a support stands ("over, where no standing support stands in the
band there and every standing span above the band clears the wall's top …; else merged"), so an aqueduct whose channel
runs above the wall's top was "merged" at a pier in the band, or under a span lower than the wall: a crossing in neither
of the owner's forms. The narrowest change that restores the rule: law 1 (2) takes the form the level gives — over where
the channel runs above the wall's top (no standing support in the band, every standing span over it clearing the wall's
top), merged where it runs within the wall's height — and a crossing that fits neither is unlawful, so the newer bends
(law 2 (a): a station slid along its deck by the support step's (3), its own derivation; a wall's line crossing under an
arch that clears it), else gives way (law 2 (c)) — "Nothing hard moves (J148)", as J149 says. This re-admits, for a
channel above the wall's top only, the keep-clear examples pass 5's application of J149 withdrew (*the edge*, a support
slid off the band); correction 7 now says such an example stands, and merged keeps every case it removed where the
channel runs within the wall's height. Keeping every crossing lawful whatever the level — pass 5's reading ("with over,
it makes every crossing of an aqueduct's deck line lawful", *J149 applied*) — is not taken: the rule names the forms by
level, so that reading would need the owner's word, and the program may put it to the owner as a question.

Five more findings raised an owner rule on one reading; the refined laws take the reading that keeps it, each LAW
REFINED: PRB7F-TIM-01 (J148: F-REDERIVE's widened run re-laid standing towers and gates for a meeting — never widened
now, law 1 (11)); PRB7F-TIM-02 (J149 as the program reads it, no merge on a gate's passage — law 1 (5) admits a gate
only in an over crossing, which clears the wall's top as well as the gate's); PRB7F-TIM-03 (J148 observed by no bar —
law 1 (11) and the check); PRB7F-LCK-01 (J147 (3): a shape-locked wall changed by a value edit, a re-section or an undo
— barred, the newer bent or refused); PRB7F-LCK-04 (J148's tier 2, the newer bending locally: a re-shape that cut a
standing deck before a ring — bent back along its drag, else refused). No refinement crosses J144 (no placement lock
refuses a meeting), J147's axes, item 110 (b) and (f), item 114 or item 101 R2 (a hand op with no lawful shape refused
with the nearest lawful declarations).

**Totals.** 23 decided: **8 CLOSED BY LAW** (the eight minors — law 1: 7; law 2: 1), **14 LAW REFINED** (every major but
PRB7F-GEN-03), **1 OWNER-RULE CLASH** (PRB7F-GEN-03, restored and flagged), **0 REJECTED**; the eight minors' examples
or END texts corrected to the laws (seven corrections, PRB7F-LCK-08 and PRB7F-GEN-06 one). Part L: 8,672 words before
this seat, 9,364 after (its markers included; +8.0 %, within the tenth). The veto list runs 1–23 with item 20
withdrawn — 22 in force; items 2, 4, 6, 7, 11, 12, 14, 22 and 23 amended, none added. Rows of passes 1–5 superseded in
part: 17 (below).

**How this pass closes by class.** *One term for "after":* what a commit makes **newly** — what stands at its end and did
not as it began, read by hand and at an epoch from its undo record (§7.4 *Application*, written by the commit itself)
and at a stage from what the stages before left — replaces every date test in law 1 (9) and (10); law 1 (3) alone reads
the stored *Order*, so no op that writes no date (a value edit, a re-section, an undo, a redo) and no line another
commit dates (a castle's curtain, a stage's trace) can split a clause again (PRB7F-LCK-01, -GEN-01), and the check
needs no field it does not have. *One invariant per owner rule:* J148's Tier 1 is law 1 (11), J149's forms by level are
law 1 (2) with (5), J147's shape lock is (10) — so every owner rule of the family is a law-1 clause the check runs
(PRB7F-TIM-03, -GEN-03, -TIM-02). *One side for a Tier-1 thing:* the op's own only where it lays it, newly or again on
a run its own law re-traces; every other standing one planned round, its own edit's carry included — so "laid"
and "moved" cannot split at a re-trace, a warp, a re-section, a turn tower or a water op's run (PRB7F-LCK-03, -GEN-04,
-TIM-01). *One meaning of "lawful":* law 1 and the op's own derivation, a gate's way by §4.3 among it (PRB7F-LCK-06,
-LCK-10). *One band rule for every raised way:* a wall's band in a raised way's strip only to cross its deck line
(PRB7F-LCK-05, -GEN-08). *One query, two-sided:* every part a commit touched and every part of the other side it meets
(PRB7F-GEN-02). *Law 2's order unchanged*, (a) moving only what the op lays — never a standing deck's end
(PRB7F-LCK-04); *law 3's total order unchanged*, its empty first-key rank struck; a hand op with no lawful shape refused
(law 2 (c)), an undo exact or refused with its dependents, a lock among them (law 4), a warp one op that never moves a
gate it carries further (*A warp*, veto 22). *A commit per path* names its stages by §3.3's `StageId`, so every clause
binds at the end of the stage whose providers have run. *Carried way*'s gate is one record with one class, one place,
one count (PRB7F-LCK-02, -LCK-07, -GEN-05).

PRB7B-09's rungs (F-WALL's and F-V-RAISED's each alone, F-REDERIVE's waiting) stand; PRB7E-LCK-09, PRB7E-GEN-12 and
PRB7-19's REJECT stand.

**Verdict.** With these refinements the laws are complete and determinate for every case the three lenses ran, and no
refinement adds a case: each major closes by one definition, one clause or one order, and Part L grew by 692 words
(8.0 %), most of them markers and the one new clause. The classes the pass raised close at once. No clause of law 1 but
(3) reads a date, so neither an op that writes none (a value edit, a re-section, an undo, a redo) nor a line another
commit dates (a castle's curtain, a stage's trace) can split law 1 (9) or (10) again, and the check reads only what
the commit leaves, its event and its undo record or the stage before. Every owner rule of the family is now a law-1
clause the check runs — J148's Tier 1 in (11), J149's forms by level in (2) with (5), J147's shape lock in (10) — and
the check queries both sides of every meeting a commit can make, so a build that moves an unheld gate, merges a high
aqueduct or misses an untouched locked ring fails §5.3's row and T110-4. One rule says which Tier-1 things an op may
place (those it lays, newly or again by its own law) and every other is planned round, so "laid" and "moved" cannot
split at a re-trace, a warp, a re-section, a turn tower or a water op's run; "lawful" means law 1 and the op's own
derivation, so a gate's way and an aqueduct's slide follow from one word; a raised way's strip holds a wall's band only
crossing, for every kind; a re-shape cuts no standing deck; and *Carried way*'s gate has one class, one place, one
count and one lifetime. The one owner-rule clash — law 1 (2)'s merged form taken past the owner's level — is restored by
the narrowest change and flagged. A further probe should attack the new texts: the *newly* comparison (a line moved
within a strip, a lock and a meeting in one commit, a stage that re-lays what an earlier stage left); law 1 (11)'s
exceptions op by op (which ops' own law re-traces a run — a repair's new stretch, a terrain stroke's re-anchor, a
contraction — and what a castle's grip or a warp carries); the level test (a sloping channel across a wide band, a
channel at the wall's very top, a wall's top changed by decay or a re-section beneath a standing arch, a frame growth's
end span over a high crossing); and the two-sided query on every path that keeps an undo record.

## Part P — Pass 7: the laws probe (J146)

`coherence/adjudicate-r7-laws5` (2026-10-02) decides probe pass 7 — the laws probe's fourth pass, three lenses that
attacked Part L as pass 6 left it: `probe-r7g-time.md` (5: PRB7G-TIM-01 – 05), `probe-r7g-locks.md` (7: PRB7G-LCK-01 –
07) and `probe-r7g-gen.md` (6: PRB7G-GEN-01 – 06); 18 findings, 0 blockers, 14 major, 4 minor (majors per pass: 31, 29,
19, 15, 14). It works under the owner's J150 (PROGRESS J150, read whole; the owner's choice of the program's proposal,
its texts the program's, J122): Part L keeps one plain rule for a shape lock — no op makes or deepens a meeting with a
shape-locked thing (by hand refused, at generation not built or the stage's ladder, at an epoch it waits) —, how a lock
on either axis behaves under an undo, a redo, a re-grade or value edit within a standing meeting, and a repair is
designed once in round 8 (Part A's hand-off, widened), and Part L must not grow. Three classes are each closed once:
PRB7G-TIM-03 with PRB7G-GEN-02 (a Tier-1 thing laid or fixed earlier in the event), PRB7G-LCK-04 with PRB7G-GEN-05 (the
several raised ways' union), and PRB7G-GEN-03 with PRB7G-TIM-01's wall's top (one term). Part L is amended in place by
class — one term, one order, one clause each, never a case list —, each change marked "(J146, PRB7G-…)" or "(J150)":
**CLOSED BY LAW n** where the laws as written decide the case uniquely; **LAW REFINED** where Part L now carries the
refinement quoted; **OWNER-RULE CLASH** where a law crossed an owner rule, ruled by the narrowest change and flagged
here; **REJECTED** with the reason; **HANDED OFF (J150)** where the finding turns only on lock behaviour under an undo,
a redo, a re-grade or a repair. No owner rule is changed, and every reading of the owner's words stays the program's
(J122, vetoable).

**Owner-rule clashes (flagged at the head, as J146's grading asks): one — PRB7G-GEN-01.** *Carried way* (PRB7D-GEN-07,
kept by PRB7F-GEN-05) said of a carried way standing before a stage "the way never moved, else law 2 (c)", and law 2 (b)
deferred to it ("a carried way as *Carried way* says"). So at a regeneration a raised road the user drew and did not
lock — which J147 (1), the owner's rule, says "moves with the settlement" — was held where it lies, the stage going to
law 2 (c) (F-WALL's rung, else unwalled) rather than the way moving; and a placement-locked one was held in its course,
which J144 ("absolutely change the shape to be cohesive and coherent, despite being locked") lets a meeting change. The
narrowest change that restores both rules: "never moved" is struck, and a carried way, a Tier-3 way, is re-routed by
law 2 (b) through a gate of that wall a way of its class may pass, as law 2's order already does for every Tier-3 way —
only a shape-locked one held (J147 (3)) —, else law 2 (c). The same sentence closes the finding's two determinacy gaps
(where the stage-1 gate stands, and its order among S3 (4)'s forced picks) by one rule for every stage: no forced pick,
the gate where the stage's boundary itself crosses the way's ground course, at stage 1 beside `N₁` as the harbour gate
is (E-D18 and E-P15 conform the counts).

Four more findings raised an owner rule on one reading; the refined laws take the reading that keeps it: PRB7G-TIM-01
(item 114: a run beneath a merged pier that decays read as "neither form" and waiting for ever — the form now read from
the records whatever either side's condition, LAW REFINED); PRB7G-GEN-04 (J149's OVER, "may pass over a gate … the gate
standing under the arch": a crossing rule binding an aqueduct over an older gate's way would narrow it — the rule binds a
way laid after the raised way only, LAW REFINED); PRB7G-TIM-03 (J148 observed by no bar inside an epoch — law 1 (11)
holds what an earlier step laid, LAW REFINED); PRB7G-LCK-05 (J147 (3): a repair capping a run shape-locked while its span
lay fallen — HANDED OFF (J150), the one plain rule meanwhile refusing or waiting the repair, so J147 (3) holds). No
refinement crosses J144 (no placement lock refuses a meeting), J147's axes, J148's tiers, J149's forms, item 110 (b) and
(f), item 114 or item 101 R2 (a hand op with no lawful shape refused with the nearest lawful declarations).

**Totals.** 18 decided: **4 CLOSED BY LAW** (the four minors — law 1: 2; law 2: 1; law 4: 1), **10 LAW REFINED**,
**1 OWNER-RULE CLASH** (PRB7G-GEN-01, restored and flagged), **0 REJECTED**, **3 HANDED OFF (J150)** (PRB7G-LCK-01,
-02, -05); the four minors' examples or END texts corrected to the laws. Part L: 9,364 words before this seat, 9,357
after (its markers included, counted as pass 6 counted them) — every word the refinements add paid for by merging or
striking clauses: the clauses that defined a lock's behaviour under an undo, a redo, a re-grade and a repair (law 1 (9)'s
re-lock gloss, (10)'s condition and frame-growth exceptions, *Entry*'s, *held*'s, *The soft readings*' and (G2)'s
restatements of the shape lock, veto 10's and 23's undo and redo) replaced by law 1 (10)'s one rule and the round-8
hand-off; the preamble's pass-by-pass history, correction 6's restatement of Part A, the check's restatement of
T110-4's round trip, law 4's op list and withdrawn veto 20's gloss merged. The veto list runs 1–23 with item 20 withdrawn —
22 in force; items 6, 10, 12, 16 and 23 amended (20's gloss shortened), none added. New END lines: E-D18, E-D19 and
E-P15. Rows of passes 1–6 superseded in part: 19 (below).

**How this pass closes by class.** *One rule for a shape lock (J150):* law 1 (10) — no op makes or deepens a meeting
with a shape-held thing — read as written for every op, so no undo, redo, re-grade, value edit or repair can split it,
and how a lock should behave under those ops is one design in round 8 (PRB7G-LCK-01, -02, -05). *One reading of
"newly":* *commit* compares meetings, not points — a meeting is made newly where its two things did not meet as the
commit began, whichever side moved or grew, and deepened where its reading is deeper — and the check reads (9) and (10)
by the meetings made or deepened (PRB7G-LCK-02). *One undo:* exact, else refused with its reason — its dependents, else
what it would meet (law 4; PRB7G-LCK-01). *One held set for Tier 1:* law 1 (11) holds every gate, postern, tower and
fixed place from the step that fixes its place — standing as the commit began, laid by an earlier batch, or fixed by an
earlier step of the event (*Not yet built*) —, law 2 (a) plans round exactly that set, and the check compares each with
the record its laying batch or fixing step wrote, so neither an epoch's later step nor a generation's later stage can
move one unseen (PRB7G-TIM-03, -GEN-02). *One time for a part not yet built:* *Not yet built* is read alike by a step,
law 1 and the check, so law 1 binds only where its providers have run and a wall's section stands from the trace's
commit as S7 will write it (PRB7G-GEN-03). *One term for the wall's top* — its section's, whatever its runs' condition,
the cap a reading beside it — and *one form per crossing* read from the records, each span by its recorded soffit
standing or fallen, so no decay, breach, dismantling, landfall, fall or repair re-decides a crossing (PRB7G-TIM-01,
-GEN-03). *One reading of several raised ways:* law 1 reads each raised way's own strip, ends, zone, deck line and spans,
and only the moves that keep a thing clear read their union (PRB7G-LCK-04, -GEN-05). *One placement:* a line as a curve,
so a gate inserted on a standing run moves nothing (PRB7G-LCK-03). *One split of law 2 (a):* an op whose event sets the
line, point or value it changes is given its shape; every other searches (PRB7G-LCK-06). *One scope for "lawful":* §4.3's
crossing rule binds a way laid beneath a standing raised way, never a raised way laid over a standing way (PRB7G-GEN-04).
*One gate rule for a carried way* on every path, its way moved as every Tier-3 way (PRB7G-GEN-01). *One op that cannot
wait, keeping its meetings:* a frame growth keeps the old end span a wall crosses (PRB7G-TIM-02). Law 2's order and law
3's total order are unchanged.

Checked and standing: PRB7E-LCK-04 (a removal under a shape-locked aqueduct's span or a capped run applies — a
shallower reading, which the one plain rule never bars); PRB7-14 (a gate, tower or fixed place kept from under a fallen
span, which law 1 (2) now reads by its recorded soffit too); PRB7B-01 (the bent group's moves on the union); PRB7C-GEN-01
and PRB7B-03 (a castle's siting at generation a search over claims law 1 admits as S7 will build them); PRB7F-LCK-04 (a
re-shape lays no end); PRB7-15 and R7-04 (c) (a frame growth past a bound keeping a pier in water or a tall pier,
reported); PRB7F-GEN-04 (S7's turn and spacing towers its own); PRB7F-LCK-07 (the nearest gate a way of its class may
pass); PRB7E-GEN-02's *Order* by the fixing step (law 1 (3) alone reads it); PRB7-19's REJECT. *J147 applied*'s clause 4
(law 1 (10)) is read through law 1 (10)'s one rule; *J149 applied*'s forms are read through law 1 (2)'s records.

**Verdict.** With these refinements the laws are complete and determinate for every case the three lenses ran, and
Part L did not grow (9,364 → 9,357 words). Each major closes by one definition, one clause or one order, and the class
it belongs to with it: J150's one rule for a shape lock, read as written for every op, means no undo, redo, re-grade,
value edit or repair can split law 1 (10) again, the lock's behaviour under those ops being one design in round 8;
*newly* compares meetings, so a line moved within a strip and a strip grown onto a line each give one outcome; an undo
is exact or refused with its reason; one held set for Tier 1 — from the step that fixes a thing's place — closes the
epoch member and the generation member of J148's timing at once, the check reading the record that fixed it; *Not yet
built* read alike by a step, law 1 and the check means law 1 binds only where its providers have run; one term for the
wall's top and a form read from the records end every split of law 1 (2) by condition or by S7's timing; reading
several raised ways one by one in law 1 and as their union only for the moves that clear them ends every union split;
and a carried way's gate, a castle's grip, a gate's way under an arch and a frame growth's end span each get one rule.
The one owner-rule clash — a drawn carried way held unmoved, against J147 (1) and J144 — is restored by the narrowest
change and flagged. A further probe should attack the new texts: the meeting-by-meeting *newly* (a line that leaves one
span's strip of a raised way for another's in one commit; a meeting made and unmade inside one epoch); the held set's
"fixed by an earlier step" at a regeneration and New random (which step fixes what a kept stage keeps); the form read
from the records where a merged arch is rebuilt as a new raised part (C10) or the wall is re-sectioned (W14); a carried
way's gate where the boundary crosses its ramp or foot, and its re-route at stage 1; the given-or-searched rule on an
op whose event sets some values and leaves others to search (a points-form `drawWall` with a dragged pin, a hand
`contractWall`); and a frame growth's extension beyond the old foot meeting a wall the same growth lays.

## Part I — canon changes (appended to canon.md as one dated ruling)

The dated ruling "2026-10-02 (adjudication round 7, family-only under J144)" at the end of
`docs/validation/coherence/canon.md` records, by pointer to the rulings above: the family round's scope and supersessions
(R7-01); the owner's lock rule at §2.7 as **the lock sentence** — a lock, pin or declaration holds a similar structure's
placement, never its shape where a wall and a raised way meet, with the guards (G1) local, (G2) shown (the fate
*reshaped*, the report entry `reshaped`) and (G3) no lawful local shape (`refuse.raised.meeting`; not built or waiting),
buildings held and a wall giving way to a held building in an arch vault; **the bent-group sentence** (lawful crossings
only inside an under-deck strip); **the fixed-place sentence** (water gates, anchors and weld vertices kept out of the
fixed-place zone by whichever comes later); both tables complete over every wall event, raised-way state, path and lock,
each cell's test holding its case through **the state sweep** (R7-02, J144 binding, its readings J122 and vetoable,
superseding R6-02's (D5) parenthesis, C8, the bent-group list, W2's, W8's and W13's cells and the way-and-channel table's
rows (c) and (e) where R7-02 rewrites them, UC18 for a wall over an arch vault, and V06 §3.19 (5)'s impassable list for
held walls and ground ways); generation order (R7-03); the landfall's ladder, the absorbed ring's vault, the frame
